# Full feature parity — gap list and roadmap

**Date:** 2026-08-22
**Status:** Planning document, for review
**Sources:** `.superpowers/sdd/parity-posterizarr-inventory.md` (67 in-scope-or-decidable
gap rows out of ~110 features), `.superpowers/sdd/parity-kometa-inventory.md` (134 out of
~190), `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-20-autoposter-design.md` §6/§6a, and the shipped
codebase under `src/autoposter/`.

## Scope decisions (locked)

These bound every row and phase below. They were decided by the user, not inferred.

- **Capability parity, not config compatibility.** autoposter's own config and UI must
  be able to achieve every in-scope outcome. Kometa/Posterizarr YAML is never parsed and
  no importer is planned. Kometa's template/DSL machinery is superseded by design; the
  capabilities its defaults files deliver (overlay families, collection packs) are in
  scope as capabilities.
- **External services: only what the user uses today** — TMDB, IMDb datasets, MDBList,
  TVDB, Fanart.tv, plus the self-hosted stack (Plex, Radarr, Sonarr, and — new —
  Tracearr). TMDB-backed builders (discover, charts, lists) and MDBList lists are in.
  Trakt, Letterboxd, AniDB/AniList/MAL, and anything needing a new account or OAuth are
  out — Appendix A prices what re-scoping each would cost.
- **Playlists: in. Notifications: in.**
- **Cutover-critical, sequenced first:** the user's Posterizarr config POSTs run
  notifications to an n8n webhook (`kometa-trigger`) that chains downstream automation.
  Without an equivalent, that chain dies silently the day the old tools are switched
  off. This is the strongest sequencing constraint in the roadmap: a parity feature that
  gates turning Posterizarr/Kometa off at all.
- **Tautulli is retired by user decision.** The user is deploying
  [Tracearr](https://github.com/connorgallopo/Tracearr) instead in the coming days, so
  the design doc's never-built Tautulli intake and Kometa's Tautulli builders are
  removed from the gap list rather than carried — Tracearr's outbound custom webhooks
  and read-only REST API (both verified from its repo and tracearr.com; nothing beyond
  that is verified) take over both roles.
- **Assumed done:** the full-pass trigger (`POST /api/sweep` + dashboard button),
  in flight on `feat/full-pass-trigger`.

Two spec-level non-goals stay non-goals throughout: Jellyfin/Emby support and Kometa's
`add_missing` family. Where this roadmap includes something the 2026-08-20 spec listed
as a non-goal (clearart, Discord notifications), the full-parity decision supersedes
the spec and the row says so.

endpoints + two UI pages; phases 2 and 3: the largest shipped units, multi-week each).

## Part 1 — Gap list, easiest first

Every in-scope missing/partial/unknown row from both inventories appears below or in
Appendix A; the completeness pass and counts are in the footer. Rows marked
**(verify)** carry an inventory "unknown" forward as a named verification task instead
of resolving it by assumption. Config impact: **exercised** = the user's real config
exercises it today; **parity-only** = present in the tools but disabled/unused;
**spec §6** = owed by the autoposter design spec itself. Rows carrying
**answered 5b:** were verified in phase 5b; the file:line evidence behind each
one-liner is in the `.superpowers/sdd/p5b-task-{1,2,3}-report.md` and
`p5b-close-report.md` files.

| # | Gap | What it is | Difficulty | Config impact | Depends on |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Smart vs dumb CS collections (verify) | Confirm whether `buckets.py` creates Plex-native smart collections or reconciled dumb ones. **answered 5b:** Plex-native smart — created with `smart=True` and live `contentRating` filters (`reconcile.py:364-374`); no membership is ever maintained. **4c/defaults-catalog note, recorded because a picker made it visible:** the five US certification buckets have **no master boolean** — there is no `collections.cs_buckets` field or anything like it, and `sources.default_definitions` builds the family unconditionally (`config/schema.py`, `collections/sources.py`). The only switch near them is `collections.separators`, which owns the blank `"Ratings Collections"` divider and nothing else, so that is what the catalog's Content Ratings row is honestly backed by and what its name says (`Preset(key="content_ratings_divider", setting="collections.separators")`). Deliberate, not an oversight: inventing a `cs_buckets` setting to give the picker a tidier row would have been exactly the fabricated path the catalog's provenance checks exist to catch. Making the family toggleable is new config surface and a decision nobody has asked for yet (`.superpowers/sdd/task-3-report.md` §2) | S — read code + one Plex call | exercised | — |

| 12 | `FollowSymlink` semantics (verify) | Whether security-driven symlink resolution conflicts with opt-in symlinked asset trees. **answered 5b:** no conflict — path derivation is purely lexical (`naming.py:17-36` compares string segments, never resolves), so symlinked library or asset mounts render fine; the two places that do resolve do it to *both* sides symmetrically (artwork serving, `api/artwork.py:89-92`; cleanup scan, `scheduler/jobs.py:344-367`), so a symlinked `assets_root` stays self-consistent. The one deliberate refusal: the serving endpoint rejects an in-tree symlink whose target escapes the resolved root (`api/artwork.py:79-92`) — a security posture, not a render-path behaviour. Not exercised: `FollowSymlink: "false"` (posterizarr-config.txt:128) | S — read `naming.py`/adoption | parity-only | — |

Divergence is cosmetic: badges read media[0], which Plex does not quality-sort, vs Kometa's weight-picked best (values.py:121). Detail: `.superpowers/sdd/p5b-task-3-report.md` → follow-up row 106 | S–M — may reveal a real gap | exercised (implicit) | — |
| 15 | `name_mapping` / illegal characters (verify) | Confirm `render/naming.py` handles characters Plex titles allow but filesystems don't. **answered 5b:** titles never enter paths — asset paths come from the item's on-disk folder name (`naming.py:17-36,67-82`), and resolution raises rather than falling back to the title (`plex/client.py:203-214`); guarded by two tests in `tests/test_naming.py`; the user's Kometa config sets no `name_mapping` | S — read + test | exercised (implicit) | — |
| 16 | Render throughput without a text-size cache (verify) | Posterizarr caches ImageMagick text measurements; confirm fingerprint gating keeps a full re-render tolerable without one. **answered 5b (partial):** enqueue measured — 15,000 items in ~2s (1.83–2.35s set-based insert, PR #34, pinned by a 10s-bound test). Unchanged-item cost on a forced pass: an adopted row short-circuits on local hashes alone — overlay/font/asset SHA-256s plus a fingerprint compare, zero provider calls, zero ImageMagick (`pipeline.py:332-352`); an already-rendered row re-runs provider selection (24h `provider_cache`) and re-downloads the base to re-hash it before the same compare (`pipeline.py:367-389,426-436`) — still no ImageMagick, because text measurement (`textfit.py:92-110`, one magick subprocess per text block) runs only past the fingerprint gate (`pipeline.py:480`), and badge text is Pillow in-process behind its own gate (`badges/compose.py:129-142`, `pipeline.py:658-662`) — skipping the measurement entirely is stronger than caching it. Full changed-library throughput is a cutover-day observation — record it during the first production full pass | S — measure a forced sweep | exercised (implicit) | full-pass trigger |
| 17 | Tracearr payload/API harvest (verify) | Capture real webhook payloads and REST responses from the user's live instance before writing any schema — payload shapes are **unverified** until then. **answered (REST half — spec pass):** harvested against the live instance (`media/tracearr`, `ghcr.io/connorgallopo/tracearr:2.1.0`, ClusterIP :3000) and banked at `docs/research/tracearr-api-harvest.md` + raw JSON under `docs/research/tracearr/` — the full endpoint inventory (23 public paths across **two** versions: v1 `/api/v1/public/*` offset-paged, v2 `/api/v2/public/*` cursor-paged and read-only), both complete OpenAPI 3.0 documents (obtained by calling the server's own `generateOpenAPIDocument`/`generateOpenAPIDocumentV2` in-container — the `/docs` routes are themselves auth-gated), pagination shapes (`PaginationMeta` vs `CursorMeta`), live-captured error envelopes (**two** distinct shapes: `{statusCode,error,message}` from handlers, bare `{"error":"Not Found"}` from the not-found fallback), and rate-limit tiers (v1 1000/min, v2 **240/min shared across the whole key**, operator-configurable). Headline finding for row 79: **the public API exposes no top-N media ranking in either version** — verified, not assumed (no `sort`/`order`/`top`/`limit` param on any of the 23 paths); `GET /api/v1/stats/top-content` returns exactly the top-10 by play count but is on the **internal** session-JWT API an API key cannot reach, so most-watched must be aggregated client-side from `GET /api/v2/public/history` (records are already resume-chain-deduped and ≥2-min-filtered, and carry `media_id`/`show_media_id`/`imdb_id`/`tmdb_id`/`tvdb_id`/`genres`), then joined to Plex via `GET /api/v2/public/media/{ref}` → `availability[].rating_key`. **answered (REST half, FULLY — spec *and* live payloads):** the user provisioned an owner key as `AUTOPOSTER_TRACEARR_APIKEY` in `autoposter-secret`, and a second pass on 2026-08-25 captured **33 real responses** from inside the autoposter pod (key referenced only by env var; never in a command line, log, report or fixture) — every public v2 path, a representative v1 set, and every error shape — banked scrubbed under `docs/research/tracearr/payloads/` with a LIVE-CAPTURE section in `docs/research/tracearr-api-harvest.md`. Scrub verified in-container: 133 mapped real values, **0 occurrences** in the banked output, including inside decoded base64 cursors. **Seven observed-vs-spec deltas, all reproducible from the banked files, three of which invalidate first-pass guidance for row 79:** (1) `progress_ms`/`total_duration_ms` arrive as **JSON strings** though the spec says integer (`duration_ms` is a real int); (2) `genres` is declared required-non-nullable but is **null on every episode record** (37/37 in the sample; populated only on movies) — so genre-scoped most-watched for *shows* costs one `/media/{ref}` call each, contradicting the first pass; (3) on episode records `imdb_id`/`tmdb_id`/`tvdb_id` are the **episode's** ids, not the show's — proven by a live 404 on a `show:tvdb:<episode tvdb_id>` lookup, so the first pass's "short-circuit on external ids" is movie-only and shows must go through `show_media_id`; (4) some plays carry **no media identity at all** (2/50, both the same show — a 10% undercount for that title if dropped silently); (5) `poster_url` points at the session-JWT **internal** image proxy, unusable by an API-key client (`thumb_path` is the usable Plex path); (6) the v2 cursor is base64 of `{"t","id"}` and **embeds a session uuid**; (7) undocumented fields are present (`availability[].replaces`, four v1 `transcodeInfo` keys) while documented ones are absent (`transcode_info.hwRequested`) — parse leniently. Also corrected: **v1 `/history` is not a camelCase mirror of v2** — it returns raw sessions (no resume-chain grouping, no ≥2-min filter, a 15-second `live` row in the first five) and carries **no media identity fields whatsoever**, so it cannot be joined to Plex and is useless for row 79. Confirmed live: both error envelopes, the SPA-200 trap, and a cheap reliable tell — **`x-ratelimit-*` headers are present iff a route matched**, absent on both the bare-envelope 404 and the SPA 200; the limiter also runs **before** auth, so a 401 still spends budget; the v2 240/min budget is demonstrably **one shared counter across all v2 routes** (monotonic decrement across six different paths); the whole harvest cost ~26 of 240. The live `/docs` body was diffed against the banked spec in-container: **exactly 8 differences**, all four `serverId` enum/description injections, and **no `servers[]` key in either** (correcting the first pass) — so `openapi-v2.json` is a faithful offline stand-in. The `/history` aggregation recipe is now grounded in a worked example on 50 real records (`show_media_id or media_id` → 21 buckets → top bucket resolved through `/media/{uuid}` to Plex `rating_key` "63856"). Residual REST gaps, all deliberate: 429 (would exhaust the user's shared budget), 403 (needs a second non-owner key), the one public write (`streams/{id}/terminate`), and multi-server/`merged_ids`/non-Plex/`track`-`photo`-`trailer` distributions (single-server Plex-only instance). **One gap remains.** *The WEBHOOK half is untouched* and is **not** a defect of this row: capturing real Tracearr outbound webhook payloads needs autoposter's 5c intake endpoint to exist and be reachable **plus** the user pointing Tracearr's outbound custom-webhook config at it — both are writes to live systems, outside this row's read-only remit. Tracks with row 21. **Webhook half re-confirmed open (notifications-1, outbound-only phase — it touched no intake code).** Exactly two things are needed, in this order, and both are writes to live systems: (1) row 21's `/webhook/tracearr` intake endpoint must exist and be reachable from the Tracearr container — there is nothing to point Tracearr at until it does; (2) the user must then point Tracearr's outbound custom-webhook config at that URL and trigger real events, so a payload lands and can be banked and scrubbed the way the 33 REST responses were. Nothing else about this row is open | S — harvest, in the project's oracle style | new integration | live Tracearr deploy |
| 18 | **Outbound job/run webhook (n8n)** | POST a completion notification to a configured URL when a run/sweep finishes — the `kometa-trigger` chain's lifeline. The exercised Posterizarr path (`AppriseUrl`) is in fact a plain webhook POST to n8n. **answered — shipped 2026-08-22, this row's marker was simply never updated.** Four commits on `main`, all ancestors of every current branch: `dcdaf0f` (the `notifications` config block and the payload contract), `d233ce7` (the dispatcher and HTTP sender), `ff6b17c` (the notifier wired into the run boundaries), `93cd3c9` (the dispatcher review findings). What shipped: `src/autoposter/notify/payload.py` (`apprise-json`, verified line by line against Apprise's own `custom_json.py`, and the native `autoposter-v1`), `src/autoposter/notify/dispatch.py` (bounded retries with exponential backoff, no retry on 4xx, host-only logging, an `events_log` row on terminal failure, and a `NullNotifier` so callers never branch), `NotificationsConfig` (`config/schema.py:1206-1228`, frozen at `config/live.py:39-42`), and two call sites — `scheduled_run_completed` (`scheduler/core.py`) and `full_pass_enqueued` (`api/routes.py:873-879`), both fired after their commit and never awaited. Pinned by `tests/test_notify_payload.py` and `tests/test_notify_dispatch.py`. The n8n rehearsal against the live flow is the one thing left, and it is a cutover-day action, not code | S — one POST with retry | **exercised — cutover-critical** | — |
| 19 | Notification event taxonomy | run_start / run_end / error / changes / delete payloads; per-collection changes webhooks (Kometa `changes_webhooks`). **answered — the taxonomy shipped.** Five events now exist behind the one dispatcher. Global, at the scheduler boundary (`scheduler/core.py`): `scheduled_run_started` (row 19's `run_start`, fired after the claim commits, detail `{"job"}`), `scheduled_run_completed` (the shipped `run_end`, unchanged) and `scheduled_run_failed` (row 19's `error`, one call site — the same boundary that writes the failed status — and **additive**: the completion event still fires for a failed run, because renaming or dropping a shipped event would break the n8n flow row 18 exists for; the detail is the same class-name-only string the `scheduled_runs` row holds). Per collection: `collection_changed` (row 19's `changes` = Kometa's `changes_webhooks`) and `collection_deleted` (row 19's `delete`), configured as `collections.definitions[].changes_webhook` — a field on the definition, the `labels`/`sync_mode` precedent, inherited by every unit an expanding family produces. Deliberate decisions, each pinned by a test: `changes` is opt-in per definition and NEVER falls back to the global target (a POST per changed collection per pass would be an unbounded volume change to the shipped integration), while `delete` DOES fall back to it (a collection swept because no definition builds it any more has no per-collection target by construction, and deletes are capped by `max_deletes`); a dry run announces nothing; the per-collection URL is read LIVE from the definition at dispatch time and is not a `FROZEN_SECTIONS` entry, while the global `notifications` block stays frozen because the notifier is still built once at startup (pinned by `test_per_collection_webhooks_are_not_frozen`); per-collection webhooks are still gated on `notifications.enabled`. The delete event routes the ALREADY-EXISTING `EventLog(event_type="collection_deleted")` hook in the sweep rather than inventing a second delete detection, and keeps the same event name on both sinks. Known limitation, deliberate: a **smart** (Plex-evaluated) definition emits no `changes` event — Plex owns that membership, so this service has no diff to report, the same reason its preview counts are blank. Every event is pinned by a test that asserts the exact JSON body POSTed through a real dispatcher over a mock transport | S–M — the contract design is the work | parity-only | 18 |
| 20 | Discord / Apprise formatting | Discord-shaped payloads and Apprise-endpoint support as additional notification targets. **shipped 2026-09-07.** Two values on `NotificationsConfig.mode`'s Literal (`config/schema.py`) and two branches in `build_payload` (`notify/payload.py`), plus the 429 retry in `notify/dispatch.py` as its own commit. `discord`: `{embeds: [{title, description, color, timestamp, fields}], allowed_mentions: {parse: []}}`, where `fields` is a **generic** flattening of the event's `detail` dict — no per-event shapes, so all five of row 19's events and every future one are covered with no table to maintain. Two colours (`0xFF0000` / `0x57F287`, both Posterizarr's own constants) off the same `detail["status"] == "failed"` derivation `apprise-json` already owned; no amber tier, because a third severity in one mode only is exactly the drift that single derivation prevents. Every Discord limit enforced in the builder with a test each, and a visible `...[truncated]` marker — `scheduler/core.py`'s 2000-character run detail is over an embed field's 1024, and an over-length embed is a 400 that the dispatcher correctly refuses to retry. `allowed_mentions: {parse: []}` so a Plex collection titled `@everyone` cannot ping a server. `apprise-api`: `{title, body, type}` — the Apprise API **server**'s request body, the opposite direction from `apprise-json`, which impersonates Apprise as a *sender*. `body` vs `message` is the whole difference and it is load-bearing. Wire shape read 2026-09-07 from `caronc/apprise-api`'s README and its `api/forms.py`, cited in `payload.py`'s header the way the `apprise-json` shape already is; `tag` and `format` omitted, both having server-side defaults. 429 now retries, honouring `Retry-After` clamped to `timeout_seconds`. It is the only 4xx asking again fixes, and `collection_changed` on a family definition produces exactly the burst the schema warns about against a ~5-per-2-seconds budget. Every other 4xx is still never retried. **No test-send button**, and why: (a) it is a new authenticated endpoint POSTing to an operator-supplied URL on demand — an SSRF-shaped surface this row did not budget a security review for; (b) notifications-1 ruled no notifications settings UI, and reversing that is a scope decision, not a formatting one; (c) `notifications` is a frozen section, so a test send would fire at the *running* notifier's URL rather than the one just typed into the editor — the button would be actively misleading about the edit under test. **Zero frontend lines** — `Settings.tsx` renders a `Literal[str]` as a text input with the schema description as its tooltip, so a new enum value flows through with no UI change and an invalid one is refused by save-time validation. An enum `<select>` remains a row-217-family improvement, not this row's. **Two Posterizarr behaviours declined explicitly:** rewriting `discord://` URLs and selecting the payload shape by sniffing the URL for a substring (our mode is declared config validated at load — never inferred at send time from a secret-bearing URL), and logging the whole failing payload (our summaries carry library and collection names, and the dispatcher's stance is host-only). Also fixed here: `deploy/README.md`'s notifications section still said "Two events exist in v1" — row 19's five events and per-collection webhooks were never updated into it. Rewritten in this PR, in the section this row was already editing. One row per execution: `kind` (`scheduled`\|`full_pass`), `name`, `started_at`, `finished_at`, `status` (`running`\|`ok`\|`failed`\|`timed_out`\|`interrupted`), a `detail` that is a COPY of the string `scheduler/core.py` already narrowed (row 213, never re-derived), and seven nullable count columns. Written from `scheduler/core.py::_maybe_run`'s two EXISTING boundaries and from `run_full_pass`, all through one module, `scheduler/run_history.py`. **The premise correction the recon forced:** the row's own sizing note said "events exist" — `events_log` is a 20-site audit table and `process_item` writes NO row to it for an ordinary item, so per-art-kind counts could never have come from there; and there was no run history anywhere in the schema, so a duration chart was unbuildable, not merely unbuilt. **A full pass now has an end**, which it never had: it opens its row in the SAME transaction as its `enqueue_batch` (so every job it creates is inside its own window by construction), and the scheduler's poll loop closes it when no `process_item` job created at/after `started_at` is pending or running — deferred jobs are COUNTED and do not hold it open (six-hour DEFER_INTERVAL, no attempt cap), and a 24-hour ceiling closes it `timed_out`. The watcher rides `Scheduler.run` rather than being a fifth registered job: a job would need a name, a cadence, an allowlist entry and a dashboard row for one SELECT, would write a `runs` row of its own on every pass, and would be gated by `scheduler.enabled` while this loop is not. **No run id threads through `process_item`** (`app.py`'s `RenderIntent(**job.payload)` would raise on an extra key, and `dedupe_key` cannot carry one without destroying the coalescing that makes a full pass cheap) — window attribution is the mechanism and the served surface says so. **Both numbers, named apart (C3):** `rendered_<kind>` over `renders.rendered_at` (composites — the fingerprint short-circuit returns before that stamp, so a settled 16k pass reports near-zero, correctly) and `processed`/`failed`/`deferred` over `jobs.updated_at` restricted to `process_item`; `parked` is deliberately not a served count. Counts are stamped for full passes only — a scheduled job's window overlaps the pool's own work — and NULL means "not attributed", the `renders.size_bytes` rule, never a zero that lies. `GET /api/stats/runs?limit=` (default 50, clamped to 500, newest first) joins `api/auth.py`'s `ALLOWLIST`, widening row 51's structural keyed sweep from three paths to four; it serves counts, timestamps, job names and art-kind tokens and deliberately NOT `detail`. Retention is a clause in the existing `asset_cleanup` pass — newest 500 rows per NAME, run first so an NFS refusal cannot hold it hostage — plus the drain-watcher's own unconditional trim of full-pass rows on every close, so no fifth scheduler job exists (C6) and the bound holds even with the scheduler's maintenance passes disabled. UI: **Recharts 3.10.1, exact-pinned** in both manifests (the pinning law; chart.js would have needed react-chartjs-2 as a second pin), two bar charts in `frontend/src/pages/RunCharts.tsx` reading the endpoint ONCE on mount — the NDJSON stream is untouched, because a history chart has no business being recomputed every two seconds. Storm guard: none (C7) — no `renders` column, no `config/schema.py` field, no fingerprint input; `render_version`'s six-key payload is byte-identical. | 79 | Tracearr activity builders | Most-watched / popular collections from Tracearr's read-only REST API (the role of Kometa's Tautulli builders and its `tracearr` chart default). **deferred:** blocked by open row 17 (live Tracearr deploy + harvest, unverified until the instance exists) — phase 8c shipped without it. **row 17 update:** the REST surface is now harvested (`docs/research/tracearr-api-harvest.md`) and the design question is settled — **there is no most-watched/popular endpoint to call**; the only top-10-by-play-count endpoint is on Tracearr's internal session-JWT API, unreachable with an API key, so this row builds its own aggregation by paging `GET /api/v2/public/history` over the collection's `since` window, grouping on `show_media_id` (shows) / `media_id` (movies) — both canonical and merge-aware, so a title on two servers folds into one bucket without title normalization — counting records as plays and summing `duration_ms`, then resolving each id through `GET /api/v2/public/media/{ref}` → `availability[].rating_key` (or short-circuiting on the `imdb_id`/`tmdb_id`/`tvdb_id` already on every history record). Budget is the binding constraint: **240 req/min shared across the whole v2 key**, so prefer aggregate-from-history over a per-item `/media/{ref}/stats` fan-out. Do not mix window semantics — `/history` `since`/`until` are instants, `/media/{ref}/stats` `last_7`/`last_30` are UTC calendar-day buckets, and the numbers will not agree. Still needs a key (row 17 gap (a)) before any fixture is real. **answered row79/tracearr:** delivered — `tracearr_most_watched` (`src/autoposter/collections/builders/tracearr.py`) over a new client (`src/autoposter/providers/tracearr.py`) and a pure ranking module (`src/autoposter/collections/activity.py`), plus `TracearrConfig`, the soft secret `AUTOPOSTER_TRACEARR_APIKEY`, and two opt-in Charts presets (`chart_tracearr_movies`, `chart_tracearr_shows`). **Two things this row's own text got wrong, corrected by the harvest and by the shipped code.** (1) The external-id short-circuit is **movies only**. On an episode record `imdb_id`/`tmdb_id`/`tvdb_id` are the EPISODE's, and `GET /api/v2/public/media/show:tvdb:<episode id>` is a live-verified 404 (`docs/research/tracearr/payloads/v2-media-show-by-tvdb-ref.json`) — so a show reaches its ids only through `show_media_id` and one `/media/{uuid}` call per ranked title, made after truncating to `limit`. That call emits the SHOW-LEVEL external ids from the media document rather than this row's original `availability[].rating_key` recipe, because a rating key is exactly the thing that dies when an item is replaced or moved — this repository ships a pruner (row 129) precisely because they do — while a show's tvdb/tmdb/imdb id survives it; the rating key remains the last resort for a bucket that has no canonical id at all. An episode id emitted as a member would resolve to nothing on a Show library and look exactly like a correct empty collection, which is why `collections/activity.py` refuses to carry those ids out of the ranking at all and `tests/test_builder_tracearr.py::test_no_id_a_show_collection_emits_is_an_episode_id` is a named invariant. (2) "ids on every history record" is false: **2 of 50** banked records carry `media_id`, `show_media_id`, `library_id` and all three external ids null while still carrying `rating_key`/`grandparent_rating_key`, and both are episodes of one show whose honest count is therefore 20 rather than the 18 a naive grouping reports. Those plays are MERGED into the bucket already seen under their rating key — no extra call, no operator knob, nothing double-counted — and a bucket formed from such plays alone emits `("plex", rating_key)` as the documented last resort. Failure blast radius is split deliberately: a 404 on one ranked title's media document drops that title and logs a count, while an unreachable or non-API Tracearr fails the definition and leaves the collection untouched. Scope held: `metric` is `plays`/`watch_time` only, `/media/{ref}/stats` calendar-day windows are never consulted or reconciled anywhere, and per-user collections are out (the `user` block is the PII-bearing one). The recently-added family is filed as its own row rather than built here | M — new client + builder | parity-only | 8a, 17 |
| 80 | `tmdb_discover` | The full TMDb discover query surface (genres, dates, votes, keywords, watch providers, …). **answered 8c:** delivered — `tmdb_discover` (`src/autoposter/collections/builders/tmdb_discover.py`) built on a transcribed 48-row attribute matrix (21 shared/16 movie-only/11 tv-only = TMDb's documented 37-param `/discover/movie` and 32-param `/discover/tv` surfaces minus `page`), with the params model *generated* from the table (`pydantic.create_model`) so the matrix cannot drift from what actually validates; `region` is movie-only (TMDb silently ignores it on `/discover/tv`), while `language` is accepted here even though the narrow `tmdb_movie`/`tmdb_show` entity builders refuse it — discover can sort on localized title and order is this builder's output contract, a genuinely different question, so the entity builders stay narrow on purpose. A companion-rule guard refuses a watch-provider or certification filter missing its required partner at load; a definition with no genuine filtering attribute is refused (an unfiltered discover is `tmdb_chart`'s popularity list under another name), via a table-derived filtering-fields set so a matrix row added later is filtering by default without anyone remembering to list it; the page-cap WARNING (`providers/tmdb_lists.py::_paged`) fires for every paged caller, not discover alone. | 81 | `imdb_search` | IMDb advanced search (type, votes, rating, genre, keyword) via GraphQL. **answered 8c:** delivered — `imdb_search` (`src/autoposter/collections/builders/imdb_search.py`) on IMDb's `advancedTitleSearch`, walked live 2026-08-25 (71 probes, since introspection is refused there) against the 8b transport generalized for a second root; all four constraint objects existed and all six operator-facing filters (`type`, `genres`, `rating_gte`/`rating_lte`, `votes_gte`, `released_after`/`released_before`, `sort`) mapped onto them, matching the plan's minimal tier exactly — no BLOCKED. IMDb validates constraint *shape*, not *values*: a misspelled genre id returns HTTP 200 with `total: 0`, indistinguishable on the wire from a real empty result, so the vocabulary is pinned and validated in the builder rather than passed through — 28 genre ids and 11 title-type ids, each proven real by a non-zero total in a live probe, since a fake id returns exactly zero. `POPULARITY` sorts a *rank* where 1 is most popular, so most-popular-first is `sortBy: POPULARITY, sortOrder: ASC`; the plausible `DESC` reading returns the *least* popular matches with no error at all, and is guarded by its own mutation proof. The wider constraint surface (keyword/credit/country/language/certificate/runtime/award/list-membership) is Kometa-complete territory the live walk banked but did not build — filed as new row 148. Every existing override still wins: a definition's own `summary:` beats the biography (`engine._summary_for`), a poster file in the assets folder beats the photo, and `collections.posters` gates the poster step entirely. **The read is contained, and the asymmetry is the point:** the credits ARE the membership, so a failure there must raise (an empty membership one layer down means "remove every member"), while a biography and a photo are ornaments — so a dead or unreachable person record logs and leaves the membership reconciled and the artwork untouched (`builders/tmdb_person.py::_profile`, mutation-proven both ways). **One deliberate divergence recorded here because parity is a config surface, not a bug:** upstream sends the operator's configured `language` on this call (`modules/tmdb.py:345`, `:352`) and this service has no such setting on any collection path, so `person_detail` sends none rather than inventing a value; the code-side record is the `NOT KOMETA` marker in that method's own docstring, and the consequence — a person with no biography in the requested language simply gets no summary — is upstream's behaviour too (`builder.py:1381`, no fallback and no re-request). **`tmdb_popular_people` is FILED, not shipped.** 10c-lite's upstream read settled it positively and against shipping (VERDICT B-GATED, not a tie-break): its membership is a Plex actor-tag search (`meta.py:42`), which is row 169's machinery, so it goes to row 194 with the rest of the expensive half. **Where this row's other named items went:** birthday/deathday gating to **row 160**, whose day-level window machinery is what it is really asking for — the dates are one field away on the payload `person_detail` already reads, and are deliberately not carried out of the transport until that gate exists — and the appearance-threshold dynamic types plus the library-wide credit scan to **row 194**. It refuses the definition — never builds a partial one — if the batched read did not answer for every owned key, which is the same containment every other tier-2 reader uses: an unanswered item can no more be assumed collectionless than it can be assumed anything else. `exclude` (exact) and `exclude_prefix` (`startswith`) drop named collections from the count, both case-sensitive. **One caveat the module docstring carries and this row repeats:** those two params' semantics are NOT KOMETA-VERIFIED — the `startswith` half is upstream's own idiom, the case-sensitivity is a stated reading of it rather than a checked fact. And `exclude_prefix: [""]` matches every title, so every collection reads as excluded, every item reads as collectionless, and the built collection is the whole library — non-empty, therefore applied, with no refusal and no log line. It is the one typo in this builder whose outcome is maximally wrong and completely silent; a `min_length=1` on the prefix items would make it a config-load error naming the definition, the treatment `extra="forbid"` already gives an unknown key | S–M once the batched read exists | parity-only | 155, 180, 197 |

| 96 | Filters subsystem | Post-builder filtering on ~60 attributes with `.not/.regex/.gt/…` modifiers, applicable to any definition. **answered 9a (tier 1):** delivered — a typed predicate model (`src/autoposter/collections/filters.py`) whose deliverable is THE TABLE, `FILTER_ATTRIBUTES`: fifteen tier-1 rows, each carrying Kometa's own attribute name, a value type, the item kinds, a source tier and a note on every non-obvious cell. **Nine ship**, all listing-resident and therefore free: `year`, `resolution`, `audience_rating`, `critic_rating`, `content_rating`, `added`, `release`, `duration`, `studio` (the accessors are `src/autoposter/collections/filter_values.py`, which reads through `object.__getattribute__` so no path in the module can reach plexapi's partial-object reload; a 120-item library evaluates with zero Plex requests, asserted). **Six DROP to tier 2 rather than ship a silent request-per-item**, each on the read-only production probe's own data (`.superpowers/sdd/task-2-report.md` §1.7; 1955 movies / 284 shows, Plex 1.43.4.10903): `genre` — the listing DOES carry `<Genre>` but TRUNCATED to at most two per item (histograms `{1:175, 2:1780}` movies and `{1:43, 2:241}` shows, never three, against a metadata endpoint returning three and four; 20 sampled movies had 38 listed tags against 58 real), so a listing-backed genre filter would not fail, it would answer wrong; `label` — zero `<Label>` across all 1955 movies and all 284 shows while metadata carried one for 30/30 and 19/20 sampled; `collection` — present for only 257/1955 movies and disagreeing with metadata on 6/20 sampled; `audio_language`/`subtitle_language` — zero `<Stream>` elements across 200 listing movies (the listing stops at `<Part>`); `network` — **stranded**, Plex 1.43.4 emits it nowhere, 0/284 in the listing AND absent from `/library/metadata`, so even paying a reload per show returns `None`. Twenty-two listing parameters were tried; none changed any of it. Filed as row 155. **The operator set** is per value type, not per attribute: tag `eq(bare)/.not/.regex`; str `contains(bare)/.not/.is/.isnot/.begins/.ends/.regex`; int, float and duration `eq(bare)/.not/.gt/.gte/.lt/.lte`; date `within-the-last-N-days(bare)/.not/.before/.after`. **The 9b equivalence table is written down**: `PLEXAPI_EQUIVALENT` names, per `(type, operator)`, the `plexapi.base.OPERATORS` key 9b translates into — or `None`, explicitly, for the two relative-date forms plexapi has no equivalent for — with a structural test pinning that every negative operator maps to its POSITIVE counterpart's key (the negation is ours, so a 9b translator that also negated the key would double-negate). **Config surface:** `filters:` on any list definition, parsed and validated at LOAD naming definition title + field, REFUSED on a smart definition (the `_membership_knobs_need_a_membership` class) and refused when it names an attribute with no accessor; inherited by expansion alongside `limit`; the engine stage sits between resolution and the cap and reports a `filtered` count. No default definition gains a filter — the golden gate is byte-identical. **Proven against Kometa, not against itself:** 120 listing-shaped items and two filter configs (six attribute families, `.not`, two range operators, a relative date, a regex, an OR of AND-blocks) evaluated by our model and by **Kometa 2.4.8's own filter code**, fetched and transcribed standalone — exact match, order included (`tests/test_collection_filter_oracle.py`). It was RED first, in four places, and every one was adjudicated in Kometa's favour with OUR code changed: the bare date window had an invented upper bound at today (Kometa's is one-sided, so future-dated releases stay); `duration` was rounded to whole minutes (Kometa keeps the exact quotient, so a 149.7-minute film passes `.lt: 150`); `.regex` compiled with `IGNORECASE` (Kometa compiles bare at all three of its call sites); and dates were compared as calendar dates (Kometa compares the moment, so an item added at 09:15 passes `added.after:` that same day). A fifth came from reading the source: Kometa accepts `.gt/.gte/.lt/.lte` on a date and silently rewrites all four to the STRICT `.after`/`.before`, so our inclusive `.gte`/`.lte` were the same spelling with a different meaning — removed, and a real inclusive form is filed as row 157. Detail: `.superpowers/sdd/task-4-report.md`. The remaining ~45 attributes and the modifier long tail stay with 9b, which owns the same table. **9b's correction to this row's arithmetic, and it is not a rounding difference.** Kometa's FILTER vocabulary is **70** distinct names (`modules/builder.py:270-350`, `filters_by_type`), of which 9a's table covered **15**, so the residue was **55** and never "~45" — and 9b brings it to **53**, because it added `plays` and `last_played` to the same table (both ARE Kometa filters, contrary to the phase's own fact sheet) under a new source tier `unprobed`: 9a probed seven attributes, these two were not among them, and refusing them in `filters:` while citing a probe verdict that does not exist would be worse than the gap (row 180 is the one `section.all()` walk that answers it). Every number here was computed rather than recalled, over the fetched v2.4.8 files, and the derivation is pinned in `src/autoposter/collections/filters.py`'s module docstring (`.superpowers/sdd/task-1-report.md` §2.2) — note the coincidence that makes these easy to conflate: 9a's filter residue (55) and the whole non-music SEARCH vocabulary (55) are the same number meaning different things. More importantly, **9b does NOT close this row and could not**: the filter and search vocabularies **are not nested**. **26** names are in both; **44** are filter-only with no Plex search field at all (`aspect`, `height`, `width`, `channels`, `versions`, `filepath`, `folder`, `summary`, the five `has_*` booleans, the `tmdb_*`/`tvdb_*`/`imdb_*` families, `seasons`/`episodes`/`albums`/`tracks`, `history`, `original_language`, `origin_country`, `record_label`, `season_title`, `show_title`, the codec/profile family, the two track-title filters, `stinger_rating`, and the three `*_episode_aired` dates); **29** are search-only with no client-side filter (`decade`, `dovi`, `hdr`, `trash`, `duplicate`, `unmatched`, `unplayed`, `progress`, `unplayed_episodes`, `show_unmatched`, `folder_location`, `season_collection`, `season_label`, and the sixteen `episode_*`). So "9b closes the rest of 96" was only ever true of the overlap. Closing the remainder is a CLIENT-side job and it is not this phase's: a metadata-prefetch budget for the readable-but-not-free families (row 155, and the budget it means is the Plex-side one at row 197 — the TMDb-side budget that shipped in the prefetch phase is a different mechanism and answers none of these attributes), a row per filter-only attribute, and the tag `.count_gt`/`.count_gte`/`.count_lt`/`.count_lte` modifiers, which Kometa's filter side has (`builder.py:419`) and its search side does not — so they belong here and not to row 101's tail | L — shares its predicate model with 9b | parity-only | 95 |

| 98 | Playlists | Playlist definitions from any builder, ordering inherited, `libraries`, `sync_to_users`/`exclude_users`, delete + report, preset playlist file. **The row decomposes into three sub-phases (`.superpowers/sdd/p-playlists-facts.md` C1); 98a has shipped and the other two are named.** **98a — SHIPPED, admin playlists:** the `playlists:` config section (`PlaylistDefinition`/`PlaylistsConfig`, `config/schema.py`), `collections/playlists.py`'s `reconcile_playlists` pass, the `managed_playlists` table and its migration, and four API routes (`GET /api/playlists`, `GET /api/playlists/definitions`, `POST /api/playlists/preview`, `POST /api/playlists/ops/delete`). **The design centre was ownership, and it had to be invented rather than ported.** Every ownership, adoption and protection predicate in `collections/reconcile.py` is keyed on a Plex LABEL, and `plexapi.playlist.Playlist` is not a `LabelMixin` — it carries no label surface at all, asserted rather than assumed in `tests/test_plexapi_playlist_contract.py`. Kometa's answer is to keep no state and match by TITLE (`modules/plex.py:1740-1744`), which this codebase had already refused in writing: `engine._sweep`'s docstring says the row alone "can name a collection somebody else recreated under that title". So a playlist is ours **iff a `managed_playlists` row's `plex_rating_key` names a playlist currently on the server** — pinned in both directions: a same-title playlist somebody else created is never ours and is never duplicated beside, and one of ours RENAMED in Plex is still ours (the rename is reported, not reverted). No adoption and no protect-labels analogue exist, deliberately: a Kometa-made playlist carries no marker to adopt from, and a playlist we hold no row for is never touched, which is what protection would have bought. **Membership is a DIFF** (`lists.member_diff` + `lists._enforce_order` + `lists._members_hash`, all IMPORTED rather than copied — licensed by two contract pins, that `Playlist.moveItem` has the same signature as `Collection.moveItem` and that `Playlist.items()` is the same reload-invalidated cache); an unchanged pass writes nothing at all, and Kometa's per-user delete-and-recreate was explicitly not ported. **Multi-library resolution walks the per-library indexes IN ORDER with no id-space merge** (`resolve.resolve_external_across`): a Movie library's `tmdb://123` and a Show library's `tmdb://123` are different id spaces sharing one key, the same refusal `build_owned_index`'s docstring already makes for season/episode indexes. **The pass is a SIBLING of `service.reconcile_libraries`, not a step inside it** — a playlist belongs to no library, so it cannot be committed under one — with its own per-definition commit boundary, run after it by both the scheduled `collections_reconcile` job and the CLI, and honouring `collections.enabled` and `playlists.enabled` independently — which meant `app.py` had to register that job on `collections.enabled OR playlists.enabled`, since the job set is built once and a job body cannot compensate for a job that does not exist; `playlists.enabled` consequently joins `collections.enabled` in `config/live.py`'s `FROZEN_SECTIONS` (that one path only — everything else about the section is live), and the registration is pinned through the real lifespan rather than through a monkeypatched module. **The sweep** is the `delete_unconfigured` analogue: off by default, off means reported, `max_deletes` caps it and past the cap it refuses entirely; a row whose object is already gone is not a candidate. **Three corrections to this phase's own recon, each acted on rather than filed:** (1) the recon claimed a "pydantic extra-forbid posture" would make a stray collection-only key a config-load failure — `config/schema.py` sets no `model_config` anywhere, so unknown keys are SILENTLY DROPPED, and the refusal is instead an explicit `mode="before"` validator over `_REFUSED_PLAYLIST_FIELDS`, **eighteen** keys each carrying its own reason (Kometa's `playlist_attributes` allowlist, turned inside out) — eighteen because `CollectionDefinition` carries 27 fields and `PlaylistDefinition` offers 9, and the table covers exactly the disjoint set, which is what its two table tests assert; (2) A7's "refuse Music/Photo mixes at config load" is not implementable at config load — the config holds library NAMES and nothing that says a name is a Music section — so the media-type refusal lands at the earliest point that CAN know, section resolution, before the builder runs and before any Plex write, quoting plexapi's own "Can not mix media types when building a playlist"; config load keeps the half it can do, that a definition's `libraries` name configured libraries; (3) `Video.listType` is an INSTANCE attribute assigned in `_loadData`, not a class attribute, so the pin asserts it through `inspect.getsource`. **`sort` is refused rather than implemented:** a playlist's order is always its builder's own (Kometa forces `custom_sort` for playlists and consequently allows a playlist exactly ONE builder, `builder.py:1515`/`:1722-1725` — which this service's one-`builder:`-per-definition shape already gives). **Two named gaps, both deliberate:** (1) `filters:` is refused on a playlist definition rather than supported — the post-builder filter stage lives inside `engine._run_one` with its tier-2 prefetch, and a second copy of an oracle-proven stage was judged worse than the gap; the refusal names itself. (2) `BuilderResult.summary` — the summary a chart or award builder derives for itself, which `engine._run_one` resolves through `_summary_for` — is **not** consulted, so a playlist's summary is whatever its own definition writes, or none; `collections/playlists.py`'s docstring names it. **What IS copied, said plainly:** the ~12-line resolution prologue from `engine._run_one` (the `builder_level`-versus-`result.level` refusal and the level-versus-library-type refusal) is a **copy**, re-wrapped and with "the definition" reading "this playlist"; the filter stage below it is not copied, which is gap (1). Everything membership-shaped — `member_diff`, `_enforce_order`, `_members_hash` — is imported, not copied. **One limit, intended rather than fixed:** deduplication across libraries is by Plex item (`str(item.ratingKey)`), not by title, so a film held in both a 4K and an HD section can enter one playlist twice if the source names a guid each section owns separately. A Plex item is a Plex item; Kometa does the same; the alternative would have to guess which copy the operator meant. **98b — SHIPPED, the surface:** the nine Kometa timeline playlists as `playlists.presets` keys (`src/autoposter/collections/playlist_presets.py` — a nine-row table, expanded by a pure `playlist_definitions(config)` that the reconcile loop, the delete sweep **and** the definitions listing all go through — one composition and no second copy, so a preset's playlist cannot be created by one half and called an orphan by the other, and the panel's row-to-stored-entry mapping cannot drift from the order the pass runs), built entirely from `imdb_list` and `mdblist_list` — **no new provider, no Trakt**. Presets are data: switch one on by key, off by removing it, override one by writing a definition of the same title. That last case is resolved one way and **reported** (adjudication A6): the operator's definition is built, the preset expands to nothing, and `preset_conflicts` names what was displaced on the pass's action list, on `GET /api/playlists/definitions` and in the panel. The **two MDBList presets carry the numeric list id**, not the `<user>/<slug>` form — Kometa cites them as four-segment `mdblist.com/lists/k0meta/external/<id>` URLs, which `source_urls`' two-segment parse would turn into a list that does not exist. Those two need an MDBList API key, and on a deployment without one the pass **names the key** rather than letting the builder's refusal surface as "source returned no items". Titles are byte-exact to Kometa, `Pokémon (Timeline Order)` included: a title is a playlist's identity (`managed_playlists`' unique key), so an ASCII-fied one would build a second playlist beside a Kometa-made one. **The UI:** `frontend/src/pages/PlaylistsPanel.tsx`, `CustomCollectionsPanel`'s shape with `DEFINITIONS_PATH` changed and `api/overrides.ts` reused verbatim, and `DefinitionEditor` **parameterised** by a `DefinitionKind` (a description prefix plus the curated fields a section may write) rather than copied — the collections kind lists exactly the nine fields it already wrote, so that panel's 26 tests did not move. The scalar knobs needed **no frontend work**: `Settings.tsx` is shape-driven, so `playlists.enabled`/`apply_to_plex`/`delete_unconfigured`/`max_deletes` and the new `presets` list all render and save already. **Two 98a branch-review findings closed:** L-7 — the definitions listing now serves `summary`, `limit` and `schedule`, which the editor needs and which parity with the collections listing was the only reason to withhold; and L-6 — an inherited-empty library scope is a named refusal naming the three settings that fix it, instead of `primary = libraries[0]` reporting `failed (IndexError)`. **Declared gaps, unchanged from 98a:** `filters:` stays refused by name on a playlist definition, and `playlists.libraries` renders read-only on the settings page because its default is `None` and `Field()` keys off the served value's type — true of every `X | None` field in the config and out of this phase's scope. **No migration, no new scheduled job, no new plexapi call, no new dependency.** 98c (user sync) remains out. **98c — SHIPPED, per-user sync:** `sync_to_users` on a definition (a list of Plex display titles, or `all` behind `playlists.sync_all_users`), a second deployment gate `playlists.sync_to_users_apply` whose default OFF means every user's copy is REPORTED and none is written (the `mdblist_sync_apply`/`arr_tag_apply` precedent, and `apply_to_plex: true` with it off is the deliberate "admin live, users reported" state), `playlists.exclude_users`, and the caps `max_users` and `max_user_writes`. **Ownership transposes one level down** into `managed_playlist_users` (migration `d7a45f0c9b21`), one row per (definition title, `plex_user_id`) holding THAT USER's playlist rating key: a copy is a brand-new playlist object in a different account with a different rating key (`Playlist._create` POSTs on whichever server session it is handed), and `server.playlists()` lists only what that session's token can see, so the owner's listing contains none of them and the lookup is scoped to each user's own. **Member rating keys are server-wide**, so `lists.member_diff` and the members hash port UNCHANGED and a copy is diffed, never deleted and recreated. **`copyToUser` and `switchUser` are never called** and the branch contains neither identifier: `MyPlexUser.get_token` swallows every exception and returns `None`, and both `PlexServer.__init__` and `MyPlexAccount.__init__` then read a token out of *plexapi's own config file* — so an empty token is an explicit refusal with a named report line rather than a write under the wrong identity. The chain this service drives instead is `account.users()` (cached once per pass) → `user.get_token(machineIdentifier)` → `PlexServer(baseurl, token)` → `server.playlists()`, with `MyPlexAccount.switchHomeUser(user)` as the fallback for a Home user plex.tv holds no share entry for — ordered by cost rather than by the `home` flag, because the operator's read-only probe found `get_token` answers for their Home users too and the switch costs two plex.tv calls to its one. A `protected` user is skipped **before either call**, by name: this service never holds a Plex PIN, and there is no `MyPlexHomeUser` class in plexapi 4.18.2 to look for one on. **The owner is verified before the first write** (`account.resources()` → `clientIdentifier` match → `owned`), because "an account token" is not "this server's owner's account token" and the difference otherwise surfaces as a 401 partway through a fan-out that has already written to somebody; roadmap row 121's wizard inherits that check. **The whole fan-out is planned before any of it is applied** and past either cap it refuses ENTIRELY with the numbers (`cleanup.max_orphans`' rule), which is also what makes the dry-run counts exact. **One sweep, one gate, one cap for all three removal events** — a user dropped from `sync_to_users`, a definition deleted, `delete_unconfigured` — and `max_deletes` counts admin playlists and user copies together, so a definition that fanned out to seventeen people blows a cap of five, deliberately. `POST /api/playlists/ops/delete` is NOT a fourth event: it deletes the admin playlist and leaves the child rows for that sweep, reporting how many, because retiring them would strand the only handle this service holds on live playlists in other people's accounts. **Tokens live in memory for one pass and nowhere else**: what travels between functions is a `Callable[[], PlexServer]` closed over the token, never the token, and four sentinel tests assert it appears in no repr, no log record, no database column and no action string. **Three declared gaps.** (1) A user's copy is **never reordered**: `_enforce_order`'s cost is knowable only by reloading and walking each copy and is unbounded at one PUT per member — the ~1,700-request pass the caps exist to prevent — and a cost that cannot be planned cannot be refused before it is spent; a copy is created in the definition's order and `addItems` appends. (2) Per-library access is not pre-checked (`MyPlexServerShare.allLibraries` and `Section.shared` are pinned in the contract file but cost a plex.tv GET per restricted user per pass); a create naming keys a token cannot see fails, contained per user and reported by name. (3) No preview UI: `POST /api/playlists/preview` serves a `users` array per playlist and `PlaylistsPanel` has no preview button to render it in — 98b shipped none, and adding one is a new surface rather than a touch | M–L — new subsystem, but builders do the hard part | parity-only (user never used them; **scoped in**) | 95 |
| 99 | Per-item metadata overrides | The capability of Kometa's metadata files — declarative per-item/season/episode edits (titles, summaries, ratings, dates, tags, images, advanced settings) via autoposter's config/UI, not YAML compatibility. **answered row-99: delivered PARTIALLY within a stated cut.** A new `item_metadata_overrides` table (one row per `(item_id, field)`, UNIQUE on the pair, FK `media_items.id ON DELETE CASCADE`), `operations.item_overrides_enabled` (default off, LIVE), `plex/item_overrides.py`'s parser and pure loader, `writer.override_edits` merged beside `verb_edits`, the precedence extension `writable - set(verbs) - set(overrides)`, the badge overlay inside `apply_badges`, three endpoints and an ItemDetail panel. **The field set, per libtype** (plexapi's own capability matrix, `plexapi/mixins/__init__.py:35-70`): `title` (movie/show/season/episode), `sort_title` (movie/show/episode), `summary` (all four), `tagline` (movie/show), plus the eight fields `WRITABLE_BY_KIND` already carried — `critic_rating`, `audience_rating`, `user_rating` (all four), `content_rating` (movie/show/episode), `originally_available` (movie/show/episode), `studio` (movie/show), `genres` (movie/show, SYNC semantics — the override IS the list), `original_title` (movie only). Season is title/summary/three ratings; a field outside the kind's set is a 422. `content_rating` overrides bypass `content_rating_mapper` (row 34) entirely — the operator's literal is what Plex gets, never a mapped value. **Precedence:** an override IS a source in row 87's own sense, so an overridden field drops out of the provider-value path and out of any `field_verbs` verb for that item (the verb is skipped and logged at INFO, item + field, never the value). An override whose value already equals Plex's current value still ensures the lock — a lock-only edit when the field is not yet locked, nothing when it already is (`writer.py::_ensure_locked`) — because an unlocked-but-matching field is exactly the one Plex's own agent is free to revert before a later pass would notice; this supersedes the plan's `== {}` expectation for that case. Genres are excluded from this guarantee on purpose: their lock lives in `addGenre`/`removeGenre`'s own `locked=True` kwarg rather than a `{field}.locked` edit key, and extending `_ensure_locked` to a list-shaped field was outside this fix's mandate — filed separately (row 246), a pre-existing drop from before this row (rows 32/33 era). **`item_facts` is never written** — the override layers between `persist_facts` and the two readers, so the provider's record keeps tracking the provider. An item excluded by `exemption_reason` (row 35) gets neither the Plex write nor the badge overlay; clearing its override still deletes the row but makes no Plex call, since there was never a write to undo — a field locked before the item became exempt stays locked, a residual disclosed rather than fixed. **Storm:** `operations` is excluded from `render_version` (`config/loader.py:48-55`), nothing touches `_definitions_digest`/`_outcomes_digest`/`_rating_values_digest`, and the two pinned literals `576f88e5…a0bd` and `973b2cf3…0923` are unmoved. No test in this cut carries `@pytest.mark.imagemagick`: the badge tests are Pillow-only and every entry-point/pipeline test that touches `apply_metadata`/`apply_badges` disables artwork, so nothing here composites through the real compositor. Ceiling is **one re-badge and/or one re-render, for the overridden item** — a `critic_rating` or `audience_rating` override moves that item's `badge_values` on the same pass; a `user_rating` override moves it one pass LATER, because the live overlay's `user_rating` badge token reads the plexapi object rather than facts, and the pass doing the write still holds the pre-write value; a title override moves its `text_inputs` on the NEXT pass, because the drawn title comes from what Plex reported at resolve time. The same overlaid facts object also reaches `OverlayItemView`'s selection surface (`overlays/selection.py`, badge-family/collection filters) — a nil effect today, since its `content_rating` accessor reads live `plex_item`, never `self._facts`, so the coupling exists but nothing currently consumes it; disclosed rather than assumed inert forever. No impact-preview row (Phase E's rule: no config key → nothing to preview; the panel states "1 item" literally). The merge job's dry-run summary is silent on carried overrides — only the applied-merge summary line ("repointed N override(s) and dropped N") reports them, matching the dry run's existing silence on every other carried table until the merge actually runs. **Delete = unlock:** the row goes away and ONE write sets `{field}.locked = 0`; a provider-backed field is rewritten and re-locked next pass, a source-less one (`title`/`sort_title`/`summary`/`tagline`) keeps the operator's last value until Plex refreshes it — both halves in the panel copy. A Plex failure during that unlock answers 503 naming only the exception's class (row 213): `redact_urls` is scheme-anchored and cannot scrub a plexapi connection error's `HTTPConnectionPool(host=…, port=…)` shape, so the served detail is the class name, never `str(exc)`. **Two consequences of the field-set growth, both intended:** the four text fields are now valid `field_verbs` keys, so `lock`/`unlock` work on them; and row 86's metadata backup (`metadata_backup.py::capture_item`) now serialises them and their lock flags. `remove` on a field outside `_REMOVABLE_FIELDS` still silently skips — now with a WARNING rather than in silence; whether it should be a config-load refusal is rows 229/230's family, not this one's. **OUT of this cut, each by name:** `added_at` (row 227 — no source, no recorded semantics); `edition_title` (needs a Plex Pass refusal this row did not write); the tag lists beyond `genres` — `label` stays row 85's add-only path, and `collection` collides with `collections/reconcile.py`'s ownership model; **advanced settings** (`item.preferences()` / `library.edit_advance` — a Plex API this project has never called; filed as row 245); **images**, already delivered by `api/manual.py:208-321` + `api/routes.py:889-963` (URL through the SSRF guard, or a path under `manual_assets_root`, per item per art kind) — three residuals named and not built: `square_art` (this project has no such art kind), Kometa's `theme`/`tpdb_*` sources, and Kometa's asset-directory precedence (`prioritize_assets`) versus our simpler "a manual file always wins". **YAML import is REFUSED**, not deferred: the roadmap's own locked scope decision (`:14-18`, "Kometa/Posterizarr YAML is never parsed and no importer is planned") and this row's own "not YAML compatibility" govern; the panel is the surface, and if a bulk shape in OUR vocabulary is ever wanted it belongs in the existing `autoposter_overrides` export/import envelope (`api/routes.py:2215-2258`), not a second file format. **Live verification owed**, the same note row 87 carries and for the same reason: the four new `_PLEX_FIELD_NAMES` lock names (`title`, `titleSort`, `summary`, `tagline`) are pinned against fakes, not against a real Plex server, and a wrong lock name means that field rewrites every pass instead of steady-stating. | L — whole subsystem | parity-only | 94 helps |
| 100 | Overlay families | aspect, language_count, content-rating regionals, direct_play, network/studio, ribbon, status (**TMDb**, not TVDb — see the correction below), streaming (TMDB watch providers), versions. **The row decomposes into sub-phases (`p-overlay-c-recon.md`, ratified in `p-overlay-c-facts.md` C1); C1 has shipped and the rest are named.** **One-time re-badge disclosure (adjudication A4, read together with the storm-pin discipline):** the FIRST `condition:` an operator writes — or the first family they enable through `badges.families` — legitimately re-fingerprints and re-badges the whole library ONCE: `badge_fingerprint` folds the per-item match outcome in beside the definitions digest, so the instant any definition carries a condition, both the definitions digest and the outcomes fold move away from the pre-seam value, for every already-badged item, not only the newly conditioned ones. The remaining 36 are the per-family tail, filed as rows 169–177 below. Sorts ship complete for both libtypes (31 movie / 29 show, `src/autoposter/collections/search_sorts.py`), pre-encoded and verbatim; season and episode sorts shipped with search-tail E-2; the music sorts still wait on a libtype nothing reaches. Refused deliberately, each with the Kometa behaviour spelled out in the message: the implicit base and the `.and` suffix (one key would mean two memberships), `validate: false` (it builds a query missing the clause it could not resolve), search `.regex` (Kometa's is a client-side vocabulary expansion, not a regex Plex sees — filed as row 178), and a bare `duration:` (Kometa's search vocabulary has no blank-modifier form for a float attribute, so Kometa refuses the same key from the same list). **Proven against a live server, not only against the oracle:** Task 5's read-only probe (`docs/research/plex-search-probe/README.md`, Plex 1.43.4.10903, 1960 movies / 286 shows) ran all nine dual-resident attributes down BOTH paths — the built search URL fetched from Plex, and `parse_filters` + `evaluate` over one `section.all()` walk — and got **9/9 agreement with not one differing rating key**. Read the agreement narrowly, which is the honest reading: it says the translation is right, and for `added` it says nothing about clocks (row 154). | 105 | Collection-poster text compositing | Row 4 follow-up (5b): Posterizarr *can* composite a styled title plus a "COLLECTION" line onto collection posters (`CollectionPosterOverlayPart` / `CollectionTitlePosterPart`), a path this deployment never exercised (evidence in row 4 — nothing live carries it). Implementing it would need a new config section, the collection font (`Colus-Regular.ttf`, not in `assets/fonts/`), a compose step between fetch and upload in `collections/posters.py`, and a two-block text render; the phase-1 argv builders are canvas-agnostic (`render/compositor.py:19-45,94-118`, `render/textfit.py:92`) so they reuse, but no Posterizarr-styled oracle exists to prove parity against **answered phase D (row CLOSED):** delivered — `collections.poster_title` (`config/schema.py::CollectionPosterTitleConfig`) plus a standalone `src/autoposter/collections/poster_title.py`, wired into `collections/posters.py::apply_poster` above the sha256 that decides whether anything is uploaded. **Styled after Posterizarr's `CollectionPosterOverlayPart`/`CollectionTitlePosterPart`, NOT byte-matched to them** — Kometa has no equivalent, no captured Posterizarr output exists on the live server or in `tests/fixtures/`, and the operator's Posterizarr *config* is the input side rather than an oracle. This is parity-only-by-source-reading and the era recon's adjudication 4 is why the row says so instead of letting "parity-only" read as "parity-proven". Three shipped defaults are OURS and are marked as such in the schema and the example config: the font (Posterizarr's `Colus-Regular.ttf` is not vendored — licence unverified, and with no oracle the exact bytes buy nothing verifiable, so the default is the bundled `Inter-Medium.ttf` from row 100's C2b rung and an operator's own Colus under `fonts_root` wins), and the fixed line's offset and box (upstream gives both parts `text_offset "+300"` because they are two poster TYPES there, not two blocks on one image). **Divergences, each deliberate:** the composite draws with **Pillow, not ImageMagick** — `textfit.fit_point_size` shells out to `magick` and every composite test would then be deselected from CI's main lane, so `prepare_text` and `FitResult` are reused and the fit is measured with Pillow's own metrics, binary-searched over `[min_point_size, max_point_size]`; a title that will not fit **clamps and draws with a warning** rather than being refused the way `separator_art._render` refuses, because a divider's label is one of ten short words this service chooses while a collection title is whatever the operator named their collection (the one refusal is an empty title); and `AddBorder`/`AddOverlay` from `CollectionPosterOverlayPart` are **not implemented and deliberately have no key**, so nothing advertises a setting that does nothing. **Scope, narrowly:** managed collections only, and only the posters this service fetches or caches — a poster the operator placed under `assets_root` (which is also where `api/manual.py`'s poster endpoint writes) passes through untouched, a divider is never captioned twice whether its art was generated here or fetched already-captioned, and `apply_local_posters_to_unmanaged` is not reached at all. **The storm answer:** the section lives under `collections` and never under `artwork` — `render_version` (`config/loader.py:48-55`) hashes `config.artwork.model_dump()` wholesale and excludes `collections`, so **no stored render fingerprint can move and no library item re-renders**, proven both by `tests/test_collection_poster_title.py::test_the_section_moves_no_render_version` and by a digest comparison over the real example config. **The one-time cost, disclosed rather than hidden:** turning the gate on moves `poster_sha256` once for every managed collection whose poster this service fetches, so each is re-uploaded once and then settles — bounded, opt-in and self-correcting, because the compare is over content rather than over a config hash. And it happens on the NEXT pass rather than eventually, because the section is also a suffix term in the three definition hashes those collections short-circuit on (`reconcile.definition_hash`, `smart.smart_definition_hash`, `lists._members_hash`, each via `poster_title.poster_title_parts`, by the same idiom `lists._settings_parts` already occupies in all three); without it a stable managed collection would keep short-circuiting on `definition_current` and would be re-composited only when something unrelated moved its definition. That one pass also re-writes each affected collection's filter, summary and sort title with the values already there — the same one-pass idempotent cost row 49's `poster_key` term and phase 10a-2's `url` term each charged for the same reason. With the gate OFF the term is empty on an explicit `enabled` check, so every hash already stored still matches byte for byte and shipping the section switched off re-reconciles nothing; `separator_hash` never gets the term at all, because a divider is never captioned and its bytes cannot move. The settings preview now says so: `POST /api/config/preview` carries a `collection_posters` count beside its (null) `impact`, because `config/impact.py` walks the `renders` table and a collection has no row there — the first setting with a real cost that walk could not see. That count is an over-estimate in the same direction the render one is, and is rendered with a "~". A compose failure — an unresolvable font named by name, an undecodable poster, an empty title — reports, uploads nothing and leaves `poster_sha256` where it was so the next pass retries; it never fails the surrounding pass, and never puts an unmarked exception's text into a served action (row 213) | M — DELIVERED (phase D) | parity-only | — |

| 110 | Dashboard broadcaster hardening (CLOSED — hardening sweep 2) | 6b follow-up (final review): (a) `StatusBroadcaster.subscribe` never restarts a loop whose task died with `_task` still set (only a spurious external cancel can cause it; viewers would get heartbeats but no data) — `if self._task is None or self._task.done():` closes it; (b) a poll query that hangs without raising is unbounded — wrap `_build_snapshot` in `asyncio.timeout` **closed hardening-sweep-2:** the controller's C5 paraphrase named a different pair of gaps than this cell's own text; re-reading `api/dashboard_stream.py` at `main@c319e12` found both pairs genuinely present, and C5 was amended 2026-09-04 to fix all four together, since neither pair was wrong. (a) `subscribe()`'s restart guard is now `if self._task is None or self._task.done():` — a task that finished on its own no longer leaves every future subscriber getting heartbeats but no data forever. (b) `_build_snapshot()` now runs under `asyncio.timeout(SNAPSHOT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS)` (30s, 15x the poll interval) — a hung DB call through the greenlet bridge no longer blocks the loop, or the connection it borrowed, forever; logged and skipped on expiry. (c) the already-existing full-queue drop in `_publish` now logs a class-name-only WARNING, throttled to once per subscriber rather than once per tick. (d) the per-subscriber `except` widens from `asyncio.QueueFull` to `Exception`, so one raising subscriber cannot abort the fan-out to the rest of that tick's subscribers. Tests: `tests/test_api_dashboard_stream.py::test_subscribe_restarts_a_task_that_finished_without_being_cancelled`, `::test_a_hung_snapshot_build_times_out_and_the_loop_tries_again`, `::test_a_full_subscriber_queue_is_logged_once_not_once_per_tick`, `::test_a_raising_subscriber_is_dropped_and_the_loop_keeps_broadcasting` — one per gap. | S — two guarded lines + tests | — | — |
| 111 | Per-art-kind render version | 6c follow-up: `render_version` hashes the whole `artwork:` section, so editing one kind's text setting invalidates every fingerprint and a full-library re-render is the honest answer. A per-art-kind version (hash only that kind's settings + the shared roots) would confine invalidation — and the impact preview's counts — to the kinds an edit actually touches. Changes the fingerprint contract: touches `compute_fingerprint` callers, `adopt/`, and stored fingerprints (one-time global invalidation on upgrade, or a dual-read migration). **answered row 111 (row CLOSED):** delivered — `config/loader.py::render_version_for(art_kind, config)` beside `render_version`, hashing the shared block (the four roots + `library_folders`, `artwork.use_original_title`, the global `artwork.disable_online_asset_fetch`, `artwork.output_quality`) plus that kind's own `artwork.<kind>` subsection, plus the five logo fields for `poster` only (`pipeline.py:1229` — there is no `logo` art kind and no renders row for one), plus `season_episode_templates` for `season_poster` and `title_card` only (`pipeline.py:247-263`), plus `library_language_overrides` **projected** to the kind rather than included wholesale (`language_order_for`). Fed to `compute_fingerprint`'s element 0 at all three sites — `adopted_fingerprint`, the live render, and the impact walk — and `_render_affecting` now asks `moved_kinds()`. The partition table in `tests/test_config.py` is **exhaustive over `ArtworkConfig` by assertion**, so a field added without a ruling turns a test red rather than being silently confined to nothing; the same file pins the example config's four per-kind values as **literals** (`EXAMPLE_PER_KIND_VERSIONS`), the one absolute pin among the relative ones. **`config.version` deliberately STAYS the wholesale hash**: it is the correct cheap short-circuit in front of `moved_kinds` (it covers a strict superset of every per-kind payload), it keeps six existing test files meaningful with no rewrite, it keeps the Settings page's one-line "version A → B" honest, and it is the value the grandfather accepts from pre-111 rows. Being wholesale, it still MOVES on any artwork edit — harmless, because no fingerprint element is built from it any more and the dual-read reads it only as the legacy candidate. **No schema change, no config key, no version map on `Config`** — a `config.versions` field would have grown `COMPUTED_PATHS`, changed what `GET /api/config` serves and turned `version_before`/`version_after` into a four-way display, so option (b)'s useful half is a plain `moved_kinds()` helper instead. **The migration, and it is genuinely free:** both compare sites accept EITHER the per-kind value or the legacy wholesale one and **write the new one back on a legacy match** (`pipeline.py:1037-1073` adopted, `:1302` live) — one extra sha256 over a joined string, no extra I/O, **zero composites and zero Plex uploads** — so ~16k rows migrate on the passes that would have run anyway; the preview grandfathers identically (`impact.py:286`) or the first `skip_tba` edit after the deploy would report the whole library affected by a change that re-renders nothing. **The window, disclosed rather than hidden:** the legacy candidate is computed from the CURRENT `config.version`, so the grandfather recognises only rows written under the value the pod is running with, and an artwork edit made before the pod completes one full pass re-storms whatever has not yet migrated, once; `deploy/README.md` says to hold artwork edits until that pass finishes, and the legacy arm is removed by row 247. **The cost framing, corrected:** the compare sits AFTER the provider ladder and the base-image download (`pipeline.py:1115-1220` vs `:1302`), which run on every pass for every non-adopted row regardless — what a version move ADDS is the ImageMagick composite, the asset write and the Plex upload. "Re-renders the whole library" is true; "re-fetches the whole library from the providers" is not, for the ordinary post-adoption case. The adopted short-circuit (`:1037`) IS above the ladder, so adopted rows do pay it — but a settled library has few. **A published promise inverts, and that is a user-visible change rather than a docs chore:** `Settings.tsx` and `types.ts` both stated that `by_art_kind` is *not* evidence an edit picked those kinds; it now is exactly that, in both arms of the panel, and a shared input reaching all four kinds is the edit being global rather than the breakdown failing to discriminate — with one caveat the copy states: a poster whose real render composited a clearlogo has a fingerprint the walk cannot rebuild, so it is counted as affected by ANY edit, whatever kind the edit touched (`tests/test_config_impact.py::test_a_logo_poster_is_counted_by_an_edit_that_never_touches_poster`). **The branch is one shippable unit:** the impact walk was re-pointed only in the third commit and the grandfather only in the last, so no mid-branch commit was ever deployable on its own — a commit with the pipeline moved and the walk or the grandfather not yet would have been the degenerate preview and the ~16k-row storm this row exists to prevent. **One optimisation tried and reverted:** a memo of `render_version_for` keyed on `(config.version, art_kind)` — test helpers mutate a config in place without recomputing `.version`, and production `model_copy(update={"assets_root": …})` sites (`pipeline.py:238,265,308`, `artwork_modes/backup.py:163`) carry `.version` verbatim while changing a shared input (those copies feed only `naming.asset_path` today, so no wrong hash exists yet); any future memo must key on the artwork projection + shared inputs, never on `.version`. The impact walk instead hoists the four hashes above its row loop. Row 111 has **no Action Center surface** — nothing under `api/action_center.py` or `actions/` reads a render version — and touches **no file under `badges/` or `overlays/`**, so `test_overlay_entrypoint.py`'s two pinned `badge_fingerprint` literals are unmoved by construction and were re-asserted byte for byte in the wrap. Every test the row added stubs `compositor.run`/`fit_point_size` and none carries `@pytest.mark.imagemagick`, so CI's `magick`-less runner runs all of them | M+ — DELIVERED (row 111) | — | 247 |
