(3) **Disclosed, not fixed:** because the marker now sits on `ItemNotFound` and the scheduler reads the same attribute, it NOMINALLY licenses `PlexPathMismatch`'s path-bearing message into `scheduled_runs.last_detail` and the notification payload as well as into `jobs.last_error`. That is unreachable today — `plex.resolve`'s only caller is the worker path, so no scheduled job can raise it — and it is recorded here rather than guarded, so that a future scheduled caller of `resolve` is met by this line rather than by a surprise **served-string sweep, 2026-09-04 (a note, not a re-open):** this row's rule — class name unless marked, the full message at the pod log — was swept over every remaining served surface at `main@c312fe6`. Narrowed: `scheduler/jobs.py`'s plex_maintenance summary (the CRITICAL: a plexapi/requests failure's `HTTPConnectionPool(host=…, port=…)` text reached `scheduled_runs.last_detail`, `/api/snapshots`, the dashboard stream and the notification payload) → `<op> failed (ClassName)`; the arr_sync wrong-instance refusal (`arr/sync.py`, `api/mismatches.py` — the operator's `arr_path` and every Arr root folder, on `last_detail` and the Mismatches page) → one shared counts-only sentence, `root_folder_refusal`, the paths on the raise sites' log lines, the two downstream `{exc}`/`str(exc)` readers unedited and pinned through the real `Scheduler` and the app; `notify/dispatch.py`'s `EventLog.outcome` → class name (the WARNING keeps the message); `intake/routes.py`'s webhook 400 and parser-error outcome → class names; `plex/health.py`'s dormant `last_error` → class name; `action_center.py`'s `?flag=` echo and `source_urls.py`'s pasted-host echo dropped; `client.ts`'s NDJSON reader throws a fixed sentence instead of V8's `SyntaxError` excerpt. One RED test per site. Deferred with reasons in the PR body: `api/routes.py:960` (a frontend contract change in `ItemDetail.tsx`/`Collections.tsx`), `routes.py:2321` (the operator's own draft), the `safeMessage` rollout (`Settings.tsx` in flight), the `/api/logs` asyncpg socket-tuple scrub (a row-214-style adjudication), a `render_version` literal pin (row 111's wrap adds the per-kind literals). **Extended by the paste-echo hardening (`fix/paste-echo`, 2026-09-05):** the law now covers the INPUT direction too — the request-body 422 serves `type`/`loc`/`msg` with pydantic's `input` dropped, `_parse_bare` refuses a `?`/`#` paste without repeating it, and the five credential-capable builder-params validators stopped interpolating `{value!r}`; the remaining `/api/logs` and 503 residue is row 248 | S — one narrowing plus one disclosure decision, each with a caplog pin in the shipped shape | none (a residual gap, not a feature) | 209 (closed — the row whose three named sites these sit beside), 136/188 (the rule they extend), 207 |
| 214 | The `/api/logs` scrub's host pattern misses the scheme-less operator-host shape `requests` itself produces, and this one is live (CLOSED — sweep 5) | Filed by the sweep-3 whole-branch review (Important 1), out of T1's own ledger adjudication ("bare scheme-less hosts = the named next gap, not in-scope") — an accepted gap that stayed unfiled until this review's audit surfaced it, the same failure mode row 212's rationale exists to prevent. **The mechanism.** `api/logs.py:68`'s `_URL_HOST` is anchored on a literal `://`. `requests`' own `ConnectionError`/`Timeout` messages format the unreachable host WITHOUT one — `HTTPConnectionPool(host='plex.internal', port=32400): Max retries exceeded ...` — so the pattern cannot match it; probed directly against the compiled pattern, `plex.internal:32400 is unreachable` passes through the scrub unchanged. **Why this one is live, not probed-only like row 212's shapes.** `LogBuffer` hangs off the root logger (`app.py:158`) and `main.py`'s `basicConfig` ships INFO, so two site families reach it carrying this exact shape: the compensating-control INFO line row 209 itself pinned (`worker.py:137`, `logger.info("job %s waiting for Plex: %s", job_id, exc)`), and every requests-flavored `exc_info` traceback (`scheduler/core.py:186`, `queue/worker.py:148`, `scheduler/prune.py:573`). The branch's own fixture proves the shape is real: `tests/test_worker.py`'s `test_a_plex_outage_reason_never_carries_the_server_address` pins the identical scheme-less string into the log. So on a real Plex outage, the host and port sweep 3 just scrubbed out of `jobs.last_error` and `scheduled_runs.last_detail` are still served whole on `/api/logs` and its stream. **Why T1's review missed it.** The review dismissed a bare-host probe on the grounds that config-held URLs always carry a scheme — true, but beside the point, because this shape does not come from a config URL; it is `requests` formatting its own connection target, scheme-less regardless of what the config says. **What closing it would take, and why it is not a mid-review widening.** A `host:port`-shaped heuristic independent of `://` is exactly the executor's-judgment fix C3 forbids here, and a naive one would chew timestamps and ratios along with hosts — the same class of over-match row 212 flagged as needing its own adjudication rather than an ad hoc pattern edit. The pod log keeps the full line under row 207's trusted-sink decision either way, so nothing here is urgent, but it is live where row 212's shapes are not, and belongs filed rather than left as a private ledger note. **closed sweep-5:** the keyword form only — `host='…'` (quotes preserved, either style, bare accepted) and `port=N`, the literal tokens `requests` itself writes, with essentially no false-positive surface — pinned by the identical fixture string `test_worker.py`'s outage test puts on the pod log, seen RED on the served surface first (`run-p-sweep5-t1-red.log`). **The named residual, filed forward:** the bare scheme-less `host:port` shape stays unmatched, deliberately — it is not the live carrier (the live message separates host and port with `, port=`, not a colon), and a workable discriminator needs a TLD-ish rule plus a source-extension exclusion list (`.py:1220`-style citations are everywhere in this repo's logged text), exactly the accreting special-case this row warned about. A guard test pins the safe side: versions, ratios, clock times and `file.py:N` citations pass the scrub untouched. The quoted-value fail-open the keyword pattern carries is named with row 212's residuals | S — one pattern alternation or a scheme-less host heuristic, plus the adjudication of which | none (a residual leak on the served surface, not a feature) | 212 (same family, the encoded `://` sibling; this one is the absent one), 207 (closed — its close now names this as the third residual), 209 (the row whose own pinned compensating-control line is the carrier) |
| 215 | The collections preview mirrors the ownership rule but not the shape rule (CLOSED — sweep 5) | Filed by sweep 4's wrap out of Task 2's review (Minor 2), re-affirmed at that review's fix round: "it remains a real preview inaccuracy ... the row should say so rather than describing it as polish". Row 142(b) gave the preview a rule — it must not report counts for a write the pass will refuse — and `collections/engine.py`'s preview block honours the OWNERSHIP half of it by asking `reconcile.would_proceed`. It does not ask the other half. `collections/lists.py:243-250` refuses a shape mismatch with `shape_conflict(collection, title, want_smart=False)` BEFORE it ever reaches `resolve_collision`, and deliberately so: a smart collection this service already OWNS would otherwise pass the ownership check and go on to `addItems`, which Plex answers for a smart collection by doing nothing useful while reporting success. So a smart collection carrying our own label, under a list definition, previews real adding/removing counts for a write `reconcile_list_collection` will refuse with a shape conflict — and it is precisely the OWNED case, the one where the ownership gate says yes, that slips through. Not introduced by 142(b): those counts were equally wrong before it, and 142(b) is what makes the gap visible by half-closing it. **What closing it takes:** one `shape_conflict(...) is None` check beside the `would_proceed` call in the same preview block; a test with a smart collection under a list definition (nothing covers that pairing today); and a decision on whether the preview surfaces the conflict message — it takes no message from `would_proceed` today, because the reconcile step that follows reports the refusal once, and the shape conflict is reported by that same step, so the answer is probably "no message here" for the same reason. **closed sweep-5:** the preview's gate is now `shape_conflict(...) is None and would_proceed(...)` — shape first, mirroring `lists.py`'s own ordering — so the owned smart collection under a list definition previews `(0, 0)` instead of counting a write `reconcile_list_collection` refuses. Seen RED with the exact slip-through the row describes (the preview said `(2, 1)` for a shape-blocked write, `run-p-sweep5-t3-red.log`). No message from the preview, for the same reason the ownership half takes none: the reconcile step reports the conflict exactly once, and the new test pins both the zeros and the once | S — one check and one test | none (a preview inaccuracy, not a feature) | 142 (the row whose principle this completes) |
| 216 | The by-line citations into `tests/oracle/9b/kometa_build_filter.py` have no resolve-to-anchor guard (CLOSED — sweep 5) | Filed by search-tails-1's wrap out of that phase's Task 2 review, which demanded the CLASS be closed rather than the instance. **What happened.** Task 2 inserted a `CHOICES` block into the vendored Kometa transcription (`tests/oracle/9b/kometa_build_filter.py`) and every by-line citation below the insertion point silently moved. Nothing failed: a citation is a comment, so a stale one reads as authoritative and points at the wrong construct. **41 citations** were corrected across **three** successive fix rounds, and each round was a human enumeration that MISSED sites the next round found — the fourth pass was a mechanical resolver run by the reviewer, and it is the only one that came back empty. That is the argument for the row: the failure is not that somebody was careless, it is that this class of rot is invisible to the suite and unenumerable by reading. **What the guard is.** A test that walks the citing surface, parses each `kometa_build_filter.py:<N>` reference, reads that line out of the oracle file, and asserts it is the construct the citation was made about. **Scope, and it is a constraint rather than a detail: the NON-FROZEN surface only** — `src/` (14 refs: `collections/filters.py` 13, `collections/builders/plex_search.py` 1), `tests/` (3: `test_collection_filters.py` 2, `test_builder_plex_search.py` 1) and this roadmap (3 filename-anchored, on rows 169/170/172, plus 5 bare continuations on rows 170/172) — **20 filename-anchored refs across 20 lines, 25 counting the 5 continuations,** at filing. The frozen historical plan documents are EXCLUDED BY DESIGN: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-26-phase9b-search-dsl.md` and `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-31-search-tails-1.md` deliberately keep their as-authored numbers — their numbers were correct when written and rewriting a historical brief falsifies the record — so a guard that walks `docs/superpowers/plans/` reds on them by design and would be deleted rather than fixed. The same reading extends to the gitignored `.superpowers/sdd/` scratch, which is not a citing surface the repo owns. The same drift class reaches beyond citations: the roadmap's own prose copies of shipped counts — row 101's 33 of 55, quoted rather than asserted against `SEARCHABLE_ATTRIBUTES` — are a second instance this guard's scope does not yet cover. **The open design point, and the reason this is a row and not a one-liner:** a citation carries only a NUMBER, so there is nothing for the guard to compare the fetched line against unless the guard is given an anchor. The candidate is the citing row's own attribute name as the expected substring — `filters.py:1220`'s `:282` cites `movie_only_searches` for `duplicate`, and `duplicate` does appear on that line — but that is a hypothesis about all 20 sites, not a verified property of them, and the sites that cite a translation-table entry (`:184-188`, the episode re-scopings) may need the FIELD name instead. Settle it by reading all 20 before writing the assertion, and where no anchor exists, the honest fix is to make the citation carry one rather than to weaken the guard. A second sub-case the design has to name: the bare continuation form (`` (`:184-188`) ``, `` (`:175`) `` on row 172) carries no filename at all and resolves against the most recent named file — 5 such refs in this document, 172 repo-wide across 19 files — so the guard either tracks the antecedent or restricts itself to fully-qualified references and says so. **closed sweep-5:** `tests/test_citation_anchors.py` walks the four citing surfaces (`collections/filters.py`, `collections/builders/plex_search.py`, `tests/test_collection_filters.py`, `tests/test_builder_plex_search.py`) with the two-rule anchor the recon's per-site reading settled — any `BY_NAME` attribute name in the citing construct, plus any backticked literal with `{…}` placeholders stripped (the two Kometa raise-site citations `:962`/`:964` anchor this way) — an anchorless citation FAILS rather than skips, ranges resolve on any-anchor-in-range, and bare continuations resolve via antecedent tracking (last file named, reset at blank lines) which is also how the guard knows to STOP at the nine `plex.py`/`builder.py` upstream continuations; that tracking is load-bearing by measurement, not by argument — a naive "any antecedent is the oracle" rule pulls in ten more sites. Census asserted exact at 26 (**the row's own inventory corrected by measurement: it said 25 counting 5 continuations; the true whole inventory is 34 — 20 filename-anchored plus 14 continuations — of which 26 sit in the guard's `src/`+`tests/` scope and the roadmap's 8 are excluded with the surface**). Seen RED by the standing law's deliberate mis-pointing (`:962`→`:963`, `run-p-sweep5-t4-guard-red.log`) before landing green. **What the guard is, stated at its measured strength rather than its name's. (1)** It is a BULK-DRIFT ALARM, not a per-line proof. Reviewer-measured, single-line mis-points are caught 21 times in 26 — the five misses are inherent to the design (any-anchor-in-range on ranges; whole-segment constructs on a file whose adjacent lines are sibling translation-table rows), so an insertion that moves every citation below it is caught loudly, while one citation nudged onto its neighbour inside a translation dict may not be. That limit is stated in the test's own docstring, and so is the second: **(2) the census is blind to NEW citations in shapes the regexes do not parse** — an added `kometa_build_filter.py line 9999` prose form, or a bare continuation separated from its antecedent by a blank line, leaves the count at 26 — so the exact-count assertion guards the refs that exist, not the writing of unparseable new ones (the docstring's earlier unqualified wording was corrected in this wrap, because this close contradicted it). **The third limit is recorded here, and here only: (3)** the antecedent is line-FINAL rather than positional — the file-ref pass runs before the bare-ref pass — so a future line mixing the oracle with another file (`kometa_build_filter.py:NNN … cf. plex.py:MMM`) would attribute an oracle bare continuation to the wrong file and drop it SILENTLY, without moving the census; the fix, if that line is ever written, is to interleave the two patterns by match position. **The named residual:** the roadmap surface is excluded — this row's own line carries meta-citations about citations whose antecedent a mechanical tracker resolves wrong — an accepted residual, revisitable as a second scope decision. The frozen plan documents keep their as-authored numbers by design and stay excluded. The folded next-touch items shipped in the same task: `test_collection_filters.py:443`'s citation now reads `(line 542-543)` — the fourth live instance of this row's class, internal-citation family — `:447` says thirty-three rows, and `:569`'s bare `parse_filters` call now binds its result and asserts the default operator | S–M — the walk and the parse are small; the anchor mapping is the work, and it is per-site reading | none (a documentation-integrity guard, no runtime surface) | 101 (whose table the citations annotate) |
| 217 | The Settings page has no per-setting descriptions — the schema documents fields only in comments and docstrings, and nothing serves or renders them | Filed by notifications-1's wrap, user-directed 2026-08-31, out of writing row 19's `changes_webhook` field and noticing the generic config editor has nowhere to put the sentence that field's schema comment carries in `config/schema.py`. **The mechanism, corrected.** `config/schema.py`'s Pydantic fields are documented today only in `#` comments and class docstrings — measured at head: zero `Field(description=...)` calls across all 34 `Field(` calls in the file, including `changes_webhook`'s own 19-line comment, this branch's own instance of the pattern. Closing this row is a three-step pipeline, not a one-field addition: (1) migrate the schema's existing comments/docstrings into `Field(description=...)` entries, field by field; (2) the config-contract endpoint (row 112, still open) serves `.description` off the model; (3) the generic editor Settings.tsx falls back to for any field without a bespoke widget (row 138, still open — the `Settings.tsx:342` limitation it is filed on has no fix yet) renders it, hover tooltip or an always-visible caption line, an open design choice the row leaves for whoever takes it. **What an operator sees today:** a setting's only documentation is the YAML comment in `autoposter.example.yaml`, if they have that file open beside the browser tab; the Settings page itself is silent on what any given field does, what its bounds are, or what a wrong value costs. **What closing it would take, and why it is not S–M:** step (1) alone touches the schema's full field surface — authoring or migrating a description for all 34 `Field(` calls from their existing comments is the bulk of the work; steps (2) and (3) are each genuinely small once (1) exists. Pairs with rows 138 (open — the generic editor whose fallback would carry the change) and 112 (open — the endpoint whose contract would carry the field). **answered — every setting the page renders now describes itself.** All three steps shipped together. (1) The schema: **191 fields across 23 model classes** carry `Field(description=...)`, condensed from the `#` comments and class docstrings that already documented them — the row's "34" was the count of pre-existing `Field(` wrappers, not of fields, and twelve of the classes had no wrapper at all, so most of these fields gained one. Descriptions are sourced, never invented: where a comment carried maintainer reasoning beyond the operator's sentence (provenance, tradeoffs, rejected alternatives, cross-file citations) the comment stays, unchanged, beside the description; where it said only what the description now says, it went, because a drift twin is worse than either half alone. (2) The endpoint: `GET /config` serves `field_descriptions`, a dotted-path map beside `frozen_paths` (`config/descriptions.py` is the one walk that builds it, and it walks `Secrets` too because the endpoint injects a redacted block the model does not hold). (3) The render: `ConfigRow` hangs the description off the row's label as a native `title=` hover — the page's own idiom for secondary text, used three times already (the redaction note, the restart reason, the impact caveat), so no new component and no new visual pattern; `.config-key[title]` gets `cursor: help` as the affordance a bare `title` otherwise lacks. **Two deliberate scoping decisions.** A description says WHAT a setting does and never WHEN it takes effect: the restart requirement is `config/live.FROZEN_SECTIONS`' fact, already served as `frozen_paths` and already rendered on the restart pill, and a second copy inside 191 strings is a second copy that drifts — pinned by `test_a_description_never_says_when_a_setting_takes_effect`. And the fields of `CollectionDefinition` are described and served under a `[]` segment (`collections.definitions[].title`) although no row renders them yet: that is row 138's gap, and the descriptions are ready the day it lands, because a description belongs to a schema field rather than to a widget. **What keeps this closed:** `tests/test_config_descriptions.py` is a MECHANICAL completeness guard — every field of every reachable model must carry a non-empty description, and every path in the map must address a real field — so a setting added in a year with no description fails a test rather than shipping as a silent row. Its endpoint half (`test_get_config_describes_every_path_it_serves`) holds the same line for every leaf the response actually serves | M — 34 field descriptions is the bulk; the endpoint and render steps are each S once they exist | yes — every setting gains an in-UI description instead of requiring the YAML comment | 138 (open — the generic editor this renders through), 112 (open — the endpoint this would extend), 200 (the nearest precedent for a config-contract field reaching a panel) |
| 218 | The sibling artwork modes' bare `except Exception` still logs a WARNING + traceback for an ordinary Plex 404, where backup mode no longer does (CLOSED — sweep 6) | Filed by notifications-1's wrap, out of the controller's own review of `main`'s `fix/backup-404-noise` hotfix (PR #112, independent of this stack): `artwork_modes/backup.py` gained a `PlexNotFound` special case ahead of its bare `except Exception`, so an item deleted from Plex after its DB row was written now logs one concise INFO line, tallied separately (`missing_items += 1`) from real errors, then `continue`s, instead of a WARNING with a full traceback for routine deletion churn. **The target shape is the hotfix, not logo mode.** `backup.py`'s `PlexNotFound` clause (PR #112) is INFO + tally + `continue` — that is the shape to bring the other sites in line with. `artwork_modes/logo.py:227-241` also carries a two-tier split today, but a weaker, earlier one: `logo.py:230-237`'s `PlexNotFound` clause logs at **WARNING**, not INFO, and keeps no tally and no `continue`. The two precedents do **not** currently agree — the row's own instruction ("INFO + tally") describes backup mode's hotfix only. **The sites that need the two-clause split added, because the hotfix was scoped to backup mode alone.** `artwork_modes/restore.py:204`, `reset.py:181` and `:222`, `revert.py:169`, and two more this row's first pass missed — `logo.py:406` and `logo.py:434` (the logo-revert probes) — each still wrap their `plex.fetch_item` calls in a bare `except Exception: logger.warning(..., exc_info=True)` around a rating key read from the DB: the same trigger, the same noise. its pre-existing pin (`tests/test_artwork_logo.py::test_updater_logs_one_line_for_a_stale_rating_key`) was rewritten in place to assert the new shape rather than left beside a duplicate. `missing` counts items (rating keys), not files or fields, in all five result classes -- even in `restore.py`/`revert.py` where `failed` is a file-count and in `reset.py` where it is a field-count -- because a 404 examines nothing about the item, so there is exactly one meaningful unit to tally; this matches `BackupResult.missing`'s own item-level count. `logo.py`'s third, uncited `fetch_item` call (`_upload_one`, `:340`, bundled with the download and upload in one try) is left untouched -- not one of the row's named sites. `POST /api/artwork/{restore,reset,revert,logo,logo-revert}` all serve the new `missing` key unconditionally in `as_response()`'s base body, verified additive against the one frontend consumer (the trigger panel renders the whole body as opaque text; no field-by-field parser exists). | S — six sites, one precedented shape (backup.py's hotfix), no new machinery; plus one adjudication on logo.py's existing clause | none (log noise on routine deletion churn, not a defect) | `backup.py`'s hotfix, PR #112 (main, independent of this stack) — the precedent; | 219 | Decide wire-or-remove for the four orphan Posterizarr-parity keys (CLOSED — all four REMOVED) | Filed by settings-clarity's row-217 pass, out of writing descriptions for every schema field and hitting four with nothing honest to say about behavior: `ProvidersConfig.favourite`, `ProvidersConfig.tmdb_vote_sorting`, `ArtKindConfig.min_width`, `ArtKindConfig.min_height`. All four are day-one scaffold (`c62daaa`), added for Posterizarr-config compatibility, and none has ever had a reader anywhere in `src/` — their descriptions (this branch, `cd52398`) now say so plainly: "accepted... not currently read/enforced by any code path." That is a config-contract smell this row does not resolve, because the two fixes disagree in effect: removing the fields would reject any operator config still carrying them (a breaking parse error for a Posterizarr-compat value that was never wrong to send), while wiring them up means picking and implementing real behavior for a favourite-provider preference, a TMDb vote-sort order, and two minimum-dimension gates — four small features, not one. Filing the decision rather than either fix. **Half-unblocked by the Action Center phase:** `renders.base_width` and `renders.base_height` now record the provider's reported dimensions on every render, and `renders.text_point_size` records the fitted title size. Nothing reads them and nothing is enforced — this row keeps the wire-or-remove decision entirely. What changed is the cost of answering it: the minimum-dimension half is now a small later change against data already on file, rather than a re-backfill of the whole library, which was 11a's own named risk **Answered 2026-09-08: REMOVED, all four.** The row's own premise that removal is a breaking parse error is FALSE — nothing in `config/schema.py` sets `model_config`, so pydantic's `extra="ignore"` drops an unknown key silently (`schema.py`'s note above `_REFUSED_PLAYLIST_FIELDS`), a posture `config/overrides.py`'s `unknown_key_paths` docstring states deliberately: switching to `extra="forbid"` would make a stray key fail a boot, "a much worse failure than the one being fixed". The live ConfigMap carries 2 of the 4 (`providers.favourite`, `providers.tmdb_vote_sorting`) and is unaffected — those two lines are now inert and may be dropped at the operator's leisure — and the stored overrides document carries none of them (28 leaf paths, measured), so `unknown_key_paths`' 422 on the settings SAVE path is not reachable for any of the four. **What the decision actually turned on, and what it cost.** `providers` is excluded from `render_version` (`config/loader.py`'s docstring names it), so removing those two keys is free — and they were duplicates rather than gaps: `providers/tmdb.py`'s `_tmdb_score` is a Bayesian-shrunk `vote_average`, a decision this project already made in code with a stated rationale, and `favourite` is `providers.order[0]` under a second name. `ArtKindConfig`, by contrast, is dumped WHOLESALE into every art kind's hash (`render_version_for`; `TitleCardConfig` and `SeasonPosterConfig` both subclass it), so removing `min_width`/`min_height` moved all four per-kind render versions AND the wholesale `config.version`, and the next pass re-renders the whole library: every one of the ~16,000 moved fingerprints re-composites and re-uploads; the ~3.5 h figure was measured on a pass with zero composites, so the real pass is bounded by ImageMagick and ~3.5 h is a floor, not an estimate. That inverts the row's own cost framing (removal was assumed to be the cheap branch) and the user accepted it explicitly: "I don't really care about having to re-render all the items, and I'm the only user currently." The movement is proved rather than asserted, by `tests/test_orphan_posterizarr_keys.py`'s inverse pin against the pre-removal literals, and the five absolute hash pins it turns red (`tests/test_config.py`'s `EXAMPLE_PER_KIND_VERSIONS`, `tests/test_library_overrides.py`'s two pinned dicts, `tests/test_season_show_title.py`'s three-kind constant and its wholesale pin) were re-measured once, each with a comment naming this row and its date. **What stays.** `renders.base_width` and `renders.base_height` keep recording the provider's reported dimensions on every render — no migration, no column dropped. A resolution floor is therefore still a small later change against data already on file rather than a re-backfill; what is gone is the pair of settings that advertised such a floor without enforcing one. `db/models.py`'s comment above those columns says so instead of naming this row as open. | S — the decision itself is quick; whichever branch it picks (wire four small behaviors, or accept a breaking removal) is the real work | all four keys removed; a config still carrying them loads unchanged (pydantic drops unknown keys silently); the two `artwork.*` removals (`ArtKindConfig.min_width`/`min_height`) move every art kind's render fingerprint, so the next pass re-renders the whole library — the accepted cost; the two `providers.*` removals were never render inputs, so they cost nothing | none |
| 220 | Managed collections carry no upstream art beyond awards, charts and content ratings — every enumerated family is postered by hand or not at all (CLOSED — the default-images-posters phase) | Filed and closed by the same phase (`feat/default-images-posters`), on an operator directive: managed collections should pull poster art from `Kometa-Team/Default-Images` **where a matching asset exists**, falling back to today's behaviour where none does. **What was actually missing was not machinery.** The poster pipeline has shipped since phase 4 — three sources in priority order through one `apply_poster`, hash-compared so an unchanged pass uploads nothing, and gated globally by `collections.posters` — and it already answered six kinds. What it did not have was (a) a table of which upstream directory each ENUMERATED family's art lives in, and (b) any route from a family builder to that table. Both are now one module, `collections/default_images.py`, whose table is DATA: every directory, key scheme and code in it is transcribed from live GitHub Contents API listings recorded in `.superpowers/sdd/p-defimg-probe.md` §1–§6, never recalled. **Twelve families wired, three routes.** Franchises through `TmdbCollectionBuilder`'s existing `BuilderResult.poster_kind` seam; nine dynamic families (genre, studio, country, network, decade, year, resolution, audio_language, subtitle_language) through a new `DynamicType.poster_kind` column threaded into `reconcile_smart_collection`, whose `apply_poster` call had hardcoded `kind=None, key=None` for every dynamic family since 9c; and three families whose builders are generic (`production_streaming` on `tmdb_discover`, `media_resolution` on `plex_all`, `content_universes`/`content_dc` on `imdb_list`/`tmdb_list`/`mdblist_list`) through the one field on each definition that names the family — the watch-provider id, the `resolution` filter, the list ref. **The naming finding that shaped all of it:** upstream keys per-member art by NAME (`franchise/Jurassic Park.jpg`), never by id — the probe found no TMDb-collection-id naming anywhere in the repository — which is why the franchise key is the collection's own title and not `params.id`, and why the universe and streaming families need explicit ref→code and id→name tables rather than a derivation. **Four families in the directive's scope are OUT because we do not build them:** `aspect`, `year` and `seasonal` are GATED presets that build nothing, and `video_format` has no catalog row at all; `year` and `resolution` are nevertheless filled as dynamic-type rows an operator can write by hand, so those packs flipping READY later costs nothing. `content_rating` gets no dynamic poster column deliberately: upstream's is region-scoped and a dynamic definition names no region, so choosing one would resolve to another country's rating artwork. DC Extended Universe (and "In Association With DC") were left unmapped, named here rather than claimed: `content_dc` is three collections and only one of them, DC Universe, is wired — §6's `universe/` listing holds `dca` (DC Animated) and `dcu` (DC Universe, mapped, TMDb list `8642250`) and no third DC code, so those two `content_dc` collections fall back to today's behaviour until a future probe confirms a directory for either. **Fallback is the common case and it is silent** — upstream curates 116 franchises against TMDb's entire collection space — so a miss is one DEBUG line and today's behaviour, never a WARNING, and it is cached as a marker so a library of unmatched franchises does not re-prove the same absence every pass. **Priority is unchanged and pinned:** the operator's local file is read first, unconditionally, before `kind`/`key` is ever consulted, so this can only fill gaps. **No new config surface:** `collections.posters` still governs, there is no new toggle and no new UI, and `hosted_poster_url`'s six-kind table was not extended — the cached family poster rides the `generated=` rank `separator_art` already occupies, which is what keeps the local-override guarantee structural rather than re-argued. **Licence unchanged and not re-litigated:** `Default-Images` carries no LICENSE deliberately, per the maintainer on Discord 2026-08-29 — *"nearly all the default images are based on other work, so I'm not sure it's reasonable or valid to apply a license to derivative works"* — images are fetched at runtime and not vendored, the same posture `collections/posters.py`'s own module docstring already records for the other two hosted-art sources. Adjudications A1–A8 in the plan, C1–C4 in `.superpowers/sdd/p-defimg-facts.md` | M — one new module, one branch in `apply_poster`, one table column, one signature threaded, five builders each gaining two fields | yes — twelve collection families gain upstream artwork where it exists, with no setting to change | 211 (closed — the divider art this is the per-member sibling of), 83 (closed — records that upstream has NO people folder, which is why people are not in this), 199 (open — franchise `minimum_items`, untouched) |
| 221 | Two active presets can claim one Plex collection title with two different membership rules, and nothing detects it (CLOSED — the franchise-grouping phase) | Filed and closed by the same phase (`fix/franchise-grouping`), out of a live-run symptom an operator reported: franchise collections split across the `!040_` content band and the `!050_` franchise band on a freshly-rebuilt library. **The mechanism, diagnosed at `origin/main` before any fix** (`.superpowers/sdd/p-franchise-band-diagnosis.md`): `groups.preset_groups` (`groups.py:415-435`) builds ONE title-keyed index across every active preset, and `groups.group_for` (`:438-464`) resolves a unit through that index BEFORE its inherited parent — deliberately, pinned by `test_the_parent_never_outranks_the_index_or_the_builder_table` (`tests/test_collection_groups.py:799-813`). `content_franchises` (category `franchises`) enumerates TMDb franchises through `facts_family`; `content_universes` and `content_dc` (both category `content`) name several of the SAME franchises by the SAME titles in static lists (`catalog.py:968-981`, `:983-987`). With both switched on, each colliding member resolved to `content` through the other preset's index entry. `20ac5d9` (franchise `max_collections` 250 → 500) is what surfaced it: the enumeration could suddenly reach franchises it had been excluding. **The band split was the visible half; the invisible half was worse** — two definitions rebuilding one Plex collection from two membership rules on every pass, whichever ran last in preset order winning (`reconcile.resolve_collision`, `reconcile.py:320-349`, guards a title only against an EXTERNALLY-labelled collection; it has no concept of two of our own definitions contesting one). **No validator could catch it in advance:** `CollectionsConfig._titles_must_not_collide` → `engine.definition_titles` sees only `facts_family`'s PLACEHOLDER title (`"Franchises"`), because the builder defines no `TITLE_PATTERN` and its member titles genuinely do not exist until the facts pipeline and TMDb enumeration run. **The fix does not touch `group_for`.** `facts_family` extends its own refuse-and-report idiom by one filter: a unit whose title is already managed elsewhere in this config is SKIPPED and named in the pass report, curated keeping the collection and the enumerated family filling the rest. The contested set has two sources — `engine.definition_titles_for`'s set (every curated definition's own title, threaded in on the new read-only `BuilderContext.managed_titles`) and the pass's own `facts_family:generated:*` run-cache records (one enumerated family's titles, which the static set cannot see). The facts' separate "guard for the contest class" is satisfied by these report lines rather than by new machinery. **The same phase moved `content_universes` and `content_dc` from `content` to `franchises`** on an operator directive — the catalog tab, the `!050` band and the franchise separator — with the members' sort prefixes self-healing through the definition hash (`groups._DerivedSortTitle` → `lists._settings_parts`, pinned by `test_a_moved_category_changes_the_definition_hash_so_the_prefix_self_heals`), so no migration. **The latent location contest, precisely:** `Regions` and `Continents` are both `facts_family` and both title with the bare name, so "Antarctica" is a bucket in each — now guarded by the run-cache half. `Countries` is `builder: dynamic`, a SMART builder with no `titles()` lister, so `definition_titles` files it under its own title alone (`engine.py:1346`) and its member titles come from a live Plex enumeration this guard never sees; Regions-vs-Countries and Continents-vs-Countries remain UNGUARDED and remain disclosed in those three packs' own descriptions (`catalog.py:1647-1654`, `:1696-1703`, `:1748-1753`), which is where they were already honest. Closing that third pairing would mean teaching `dynamic` a `titles()` lister that enumerates Plex at config time, which is a different row nobody has asked for. |
| 222 | No per-definition poster URL — an operator cannot point one collection at arbitrary artwork without dropping a file on disk (CLOSED — `poster_url:` on `CollectionDefinition`) | Filed by the franchise-grouping phase's wrap, out of an operator's own workaround rather than out of a gap in the code: asked for specific ThePosterDB art on `DC Extended Universe` and `In Association With DC`, the answer was the EXISTING local-override path (`<assets_root>/<library>/<title>/poster.webp`), which outranks every hosted default and survives everything. That works and is what shipped. What it costs is a file per collection on the box, which is the only reason this row exists. **The gap, in Kometa's terms:** `url_poster` is Kometa's per-collection artwork override (`library.py:453`, above `tmdb_person` at `:462`), and this service has no equivalent — `collections/posters.py` resolves artwork from three sources in a fixed priority (local override, then the hosted default the builder names through `BuilderResult.poster_kind`/`poster_key`, then nothing), none of them operator-addressable per definition. **The shape:** a `poster_url:` field on `CollectionDefinition`, fetched through the same provider cache and hash-comparison the hosted defaults already use, slotted BETWEEN the local override and the hosted defaults — so a file on disk still wins (an operator who put one there meant it) and a URL still beats a generic default. **Pairs with row 138** (the definitions editor), which is what would make it reachable without hand-editing YAML: `poster_url` on a list-of-objects config section has exactly row 138's problem, the one the franchise `max_collections` incident already demonstrated. Three things the row's shape did not say and the work found. **(1) A prior control already existed.** `POST /api/collections/{id}/poster` (`api/manual.py:504`) and the inline form on the Collections page (`frontend/src/pages/Collections.tsx:76-108`) have let an operator install an arbitrary image URL per collection since 2026-08-24 — a week before this row was filed — so the gap was never "cannot", it was "not declarative": that path writes a file into the local-override rung, invisible to the config, to an export, and to the definitions editor. That is the gap this field closes, and the row's Gap wording overstated it. **(2) The fetch could not reuse `fetch_poster`.** `posters.fetch_poster` is a bare `http.get` on the shared client with no scheme allowlist, no address check and no redirect control — safe only because every URL it had ever seen was built by this repo from `DEFAULT_IMAGES_BASE` or TMDb's CDN, which `net/guard.py` names by name (`:258-262`). An operator-typed URL breaks that invariant outright, so the rung goes through `guarded_download`, inheriting the guard's two documented residuals (DNS rebinding, NAT64/6to4 literals) unchanged. **(3) The field had to be a ride-along or it would have been a lie.** `lists.py:325` returns before `apply_poster` whenever the membership hash is current and `poster_sha256` is set, so a `poster_url` outside `_RIDE_ALONG_DEFAULTS` would have been a setting that reads as saved and silently never applies — `_settings_parts`' own stated failure mode. It contributes nothing at its `None` default, so every stored `definition_hash` and `_members_hash` is byte-identical and only definitions that adopt the field re-reconcile, once each. **Row 213:** the URL is operator-typed and can carry userinfo or a signed query, so the rung's `source` is the fixed phrase "the definition's poster URL", refusals hand back the guard's own URL-free reason, and the config-load refusal names only the field and the shape — the URL reaches neither a served action nor a log line; a transport failure is reported to the operator as a fixed refusal sentence and logs only the exception's class name, never in the served string. **Not a render input:** the field lives under `collections`, which `render_version` excludes by name (`config/loader.py:99-104`), so no item fingerprint moves and no migration was needed. Row 138 (the definitions editor) was already closed when this landed; the field is its tenth curated control. | M — a field, a rung, a guarded fetch, a hash term | new optional field | 138 (CLOSED) |
| 223 | A refused family's existing members are frozen out of re-sort/prune (CLOSED — coupling kept, refusal made audible) | Filed by the mass-ops-1 wrap, out of the franchise-cap incident: the live franchise family held ~360 TMDb franchise buckets against a pinned `max_collections: 250`, so `dynamic.py`'s expansion refused the whole family on every pass — and a refused family never reaches its own reconcile/re-sort/prune step, so its already-built members stayed frozen at stale sort prefixes indefinitely rather than merely not growing (`.superpowers/sdd/p-franchise-cap-report.md`). | 224 | `credits_family` selects top-N by credit count before Plex-searchability (CLOSED — filtered before the cap) | Filed by the mass-ops-1 wrap, out of a production log showing ~40 recurring WARNING lines per pass from `builders/credits_family.py`. `eligible[:params.limit]` (`credits_family.py:326-329`) ranks and caps to the top N most-credited people BEFORE any of them is tried against Plex — each capped person is then run through `parse_filters`/the tag search individually (`credits_family.py:418-436`), and a person whose tag turns out unsearchable there (an empty/whitespace-only tag value; separately, Plex's own per-item `<Role>` cap at 200 children server-side can undercount a deep-credited person's true appearances, per row 194) is logged with a WARNING and simply dropped — wasting the slot instead of backfilling from the next-eligible, lower-ranked person who might have searched cleanly. Scope note carried forward: this fixes the SEARCHABILITY half only. Row 194's server-side 200-`<Role>` cap is a RANKING defect — an undercounted person never reaches `eligible` at `depth: 5` — and no reordering touches it; `catalog.py`'s "the counts are a FLOOR" disclosure stays, with one sentence added beside it on where `limit:` is now filled from. | 230 | Row 87's `reset` verb is unimplementable as the row states it (CLOSED — won't-do; refiled as row 256) | "Reset" means restoring the value Plex's own agent would produce for a field. | 231 | `PUT /api/config/overrides` accepted a whole-document replace with no evidence the sender knew what it was replacing (CLOSED — config-safety) | Two independent defects, one family, and between them they explain every observation of the 2026-09-01 wipe. **D1:** `OverridesBody` was a plain `BaseModel`, so pydantic v2's default `extra="ignore"` matched a body of `{"artwork": ..., "scheduler": ...}` — the overrides document sent bare, which is what a hand-written fetch produces — against zero declared fields, bound `document` to its `{}` default, and the unconditional `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` wrote it over seventeen stored overrides with a 200 and two plausible version stamps. Reproduced deterministically. **D2:** four pages (`Settings`, `CatalogPanel`, `GroupsPanel`, `CustomCollectionsPanel`) each seed the WHOLE document at mount and PUT the whole result, and nothing invalidated a seed when a different page wrote — a textbook lost update, and the reason the operator's own `collections.separator_style: sand` save did not stick while the identical second one did. That observation is the operator's, and it is what promoted D2 from a structural argument to a confirmed defect. Closed by: `extra="forbid"` on the envelope (the document stays lenient — `unknown_key_paths` gives a better error at depth); an empty-document refusal and a >3-path drop cap, both `confirm: true`-overridable, on the WRITE path only so a preview is never refused for being destructive; a content-hash `overrides_revision` served on `GET /api/config`, sent back by all four pages, and compared under `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` inside the writing transaction, with a stale save answering 409 and every page re-seeding rather than retrying; path counts on the audit row, so the next such event reads `12 -> 0` in the log instead of costing an investigation; twenty pre-write snapshots in `config_override_snapshots` captured in the same transaction as the upsert, with list/read/restore endpoints and a restore that is itself snapshotted; and an unredacted export/import envelope whose import is a save through the same guarded path. Deliberately no `config/schema.py` change: the drop cap and the retention are module constants, because a destructiveness cap an operator can raise from the page the destructive write comes from is not a cap | M — shipped | none (module constants, not settings) | — |

An already-correct code (`eng`) is pinned byte-identical by literal digest; `und`/`mis`/`qaa` keep their raw tag, are not table keys, and still draw nothing; `_outcomes_digest` is unaffected (overlay conditions read the C2b `*_stream_languages` fields, which already converted correctly); and no file under `assets/` is touched, so `asset_manifest_sha` cannot move. Size the population from Plex — `GET /library/sections/{id}/audioLanguage` for the server's own key spellings, then `GET /library/sections/{id}/all?audioLanguage={key}&X-Plex-Container-Size=0` and read `totalSize` — with `SELECT count(*) FROM renders WHERE badge_fingerprint IS NOT NULL AND upload_status = 'uploaded'` as the upper bound; nothing in the database stores a stream's audio language. **One regression, pinned by test rather than hidden, and left as the operator's call:** the reverse class (slice hit a real key, converted code is not one) holds 27 valid tags, and in 26 the accidental flag was itself wrong (`arm` Armenian drew Egypt's, `fao` Faroese drew Iran's, `fij` Fijian drew Finland's, `fry` Frisian drew France's, `jav` Javanese drew Japan's, `mlt` Maltese drew India's), so losing those is part of the fix. **`nob` is the exception:** Norwegian Bokmål reduces to `nb`, and the table carries `no` but neither `nb` nor `nn`, so a `nob`-tagged track loses a flag that was right (`nor`, the commoner tag, reduces to `no` and is unaffected; `nno` drew nothing before or after; `langcodes` 3.5.1 offers no way back to a table key — `broader_tags()` returns `["nb", "und"]`, neither of which `languages.json` carries either). The alias is at lookup, NOT in `languages.json`, so `MANIFEST.sha256` and `asset_manifest_sha` do not move and only items with a Norwegian audio track re-badge. The OTHER remedy — real `nb`/`nn` rows in `languages.json`, with upstream's own `NB`/120 and `NN`/110 text and weights — stays untaken, and stays the operator's call, because it moves the manifest. | S | — | 100 |

The audit row cannot be tied back to the item by any served surface either: `events_snapshot` (`api/snapshots.py`) never selects `payload`, because that column holds whole webhook bodies and can carry a sending service's token, and the `FORK_EVENT` row's rating keys live only there. So an operator pressing Re-run on a stale row got `{"queued": true}` and a job that finished having done nothing, with the whole explanation in a pod-log WARNING they cannot see. **Two shapes were considered; the events-feed one was refused.** Surfacing the latest `FORK_OUTCOME` on the item view needs the row identity, which exists only in the unserved `payload` — closing it that way means widening the very surface rows 136/188/209/213 argue for narrowing, at the site the codebase already singled out as unsafe, and it answers only after a job has run. It interpolates **nothing** (no key, title, library, path, URL or token), so the class-name-only rule is satisfied by construction rather than by redaction, and a test pins that emptiness. It says **"may"**, not "will": the twin's existence is necessary for the stop and not sufficient — if this row is the live one and the twin is the fossil, `resolve()` answers this row's own key and the job runs normally — and separating the two would mean asking Plex which key is live, i.e. doing the job's work inside a request handler. `ItemDetail` renders the string verbatim on its own line, on the de-duplicated response too, because the condition belongs to the row and not to the click. Nothing reaches the Jobs page and nothing needed to: no job column is written, so the stop's deliberate silence on `last_error` is preserved exactly. The silence also runs the other way: the note fires only when `_identity_clauses` finds a shared external id under a matching library, so it stays null for id-disjoint adopted seasons/episodes (row 239's own disclosed leftover population), cross-library re-keys, and id-less rows even though the stop can still fire for all three — the note covers the identity-twin case only | S — one read, one constant, one response key, one `<p>` | none (a blind spot, not a feature) | 239 (the identity re-key + twin merge phase this stop belongs to); 136/188/209/213 (the served-surface rule the note is built to satisfy) |

Any row that missed the 2026-09-05 pass is already condemned to one re-render on its next visit whether or not this row lands. | 249 | `smart_label`: a list builder wearing a smart hat | Split out of row 184 by the filter-parity phase, which shipped that row's `smart_url` half and ruled this one out of an S–M. It is not a bigger version of `smart_filter`; it is a different SHAPE. Kometa (`modules/builder.py:1197-1224`) resolves a membership from the definition's OTHER builders, writes a label onto every resolved ITEM, and only then creates a smart collection whose filter is `label: <name>` — the default being `{"sort_by": "random", "all": {"label": [self.name]}}` (`:1200`), a filter on the collection's own name. So membership is maintained by label WRITES, not by Plex, which contradicts the thesis 9c was built on. Four things it needs, none of which exists: **(a)** un-refusing `item_label` for exactly one builder — it is refused for every smart builder today with the reason spelled out at `builders/smart_filter.py:132-135` ("this service never resolves this collection's members -- Plex does -- so there is no list of items to label"), and `smart_label` inverts precisely that; **(b)** a builder that is a list builder AND a smart builder, which the `Builder`/`SmartBuilder` protocol split (`builders/base.py`) does not currently express and which the engine's own dispatch (`collections/engine.py:433`) reads as a single boolean; **(c)** ORDERING — label the members, confirm the label exists, then create — which is Kometa's `smart_label_check` gate (`modules/plex.py:1573-1578`, returning True only once the label is already in the library), so the collection cannot be created before the members carry it; **(d)** a dry-run answer, because on a dry run the labels are not written and the gate therefore cannot pass, which makes "what would this have built" unanswerable without a second mechanism. Kometa additionally bars it for playlists, overlays and music libraries (`:1202`) and errors on `smart_url` + `smart_label` together (`:1498-1499`). That is a design phase, not an S–M | M–L — needs a hybrid builder protocol, an `item_label` un-refusal scoped to one builder, an ordering gate and a dry-run ruling | parity-only (no caller today) | 90, 184 |
